[10/21/2024 11:20 AM] kbreezy
I believe that the ps_stock_available update is related to our concern


[10/21/2024 11:20 AM] kbreezy
Let me explain


[10/21/2024 11:21 AM] kbreezy
We use packs in prestahop to sell bundles of products together as small kits (like the configurator but simpler without options)


[10/21/2024 11:21 AM] kbreezy
We expect that if a pack is using 2x item A and 1x item B that the site will know how many packs can be sold based on the current stock values of items A and B


[10/21/2024 11:21 AM] kbreezy
This works when you first set up a pack. Prestashop will calculate how many packs are available automatically


[10/21/2024 11:21 AM] kbreezy
But if stock of product A or B changes (by adding stock or a purchase) prestashop does not update the pack quantity available to the new value.


[10/21/2024 11:21 AM] kbreezy
This module fixes that by running a hook or cron job that finds all product packs set to "decrement both" and finds the number of packs available based on current stocks


[10/21/2024 11:23 AM] kbreezy
---

This process though is apparently extremely resource intensive , and probably not optimized very well.

I'm hoping you will find that indexes can help here - because both the hook and the cron job method of reconicling some of these packs is 2-4minutes/


[10/21/2024 11:24 AM] kbreezy
The hook is run during checkout for order validation, and if a cusomer purchases some heat set inserts they sometimes wait on the "buy now" button for literal minutes

[10/21/2024 11:36 AM] instazx2
so we have the Google Sheet, that is the top 5% slowest queries your server sees


[10/21/2024 11:37 AM] instazx2
the SQL text gets annoyingly truncated, so we have to take an extra step to get the full query


[10/21/2024 11:38 AM] instazx2
the "Digest" column is a unique identifier for each query that gets executed, and we can turn that back into the full query the engine is trying to run.  we use this query to get that value back out:
```sql
select DIGEST_TEXT 
from performance_schema.events_statements_summary_by_digest
where digest = '43f777a6caa6ff2b44298256c968f6d8829f5689dae639c8b608be194f568ed4';
```


[10/21/2024 11:39 AM] instazx2
the -ed4 digest is your `UPDATE ps_stock_available ...` query, but the full thing is:
```sql
UPDATE `ps_stock_available` `sa` SET `sa` . `reserved_quantity` = ( SELECT SUM ( `od` . `product_quantity` - `od` . `product_quantity_refunded` ) FROM `ps_orders` `o` INNER JOIN `ps_order_detail` `od` ON `od` . `id_order` = `o` . `id_order` INNER JOIN `ps_order_state` `os` ON `os` . `id_order_state` = `o` . `current_state` WHERE `o` . `id_shop` = ? AND `os` . `shipped` != ? AND ( `o` . `valid` = ? OR ( `os` . `id_order_state` != ? AND `os` . `id_order_state` != ? ) ) AND `sa` . `id_product` = `od` . `product_id` AND `sa` . `id_product_attribute` = `od` . `product_attribute_id` GROUP BY `od` . `product_id` , `od` . `product_attribute_id` ) WHERE `sa` . `id_shop` = ?
```

which is a *monster* of a query to start with


[10/21/2024 11:40 AM] instazx2
dropping it into an online beautifer (https://codebeautify.org/sqlformatter) gives us a slightly more readable query that we can begin to look at:
```sql
UPDATE 
  `ps_stock_available` `sa` 
SET 
  `sa`.`reserved_quantity` = (
    SELECT 
      SUM (
        `od`.`product_quantity` - `od`.`product_quantity_refunded`
      ) 
    FROM 
      `ps_orders` `o` 
      INNER JOIN `ps_order_detail` `od` ON `od`.`id_order` = `o`.`id_order` 
      INNER JOIN `ps_order_state` `os` ON `os`.`id_order_state` = `o`.`current_state` 
    WHERE 
      `o`.`id_shop` = ? 
      AND `os`.`shipped` != ? 
      AND (
        `o`.`valid` = ? 
        OR (
          `os`.`id_order_state` != ? 
          AND `os`.`id_order_state` != ?
        )
      ) 
      AND `sa`.`id_product` = `od`.`product_id` 
      AND `sa`.`id_product_attribute` = `od`.`product_attribute_id` 
    GROUP BY 
      `od`.`product_id`, 
      `od`.`product_attribute_id`
  ) 
WHERE 
  `sa`.`id_shop` = ?
```


[10/21/2024 11:41 AM] instazx2

[10/21/2024 12:01 PM] instazx2
@Kbreezy - KB3D this is what i ultimately ended up with -- i had to fix the spacing around "SUM" as well:
```sql
UPDATE 
  `ps_stock_available` `sa` 
SET 
  `sa`.`reserved_quantity` = (
    SELECT 
      SUM(`od`.`product_quantity` - `od`.`product_quantity_refunded`)
    FROM 
      `ps_orders` `o` 
      INNER JOIN `ps_order_detail` `od` ON `od`.`id_order` = `o`.`id_order` 
      INNER JOIN `ps_order_state` `os` ON `os`.`id_order_state` = `o`.`current_state` 
    WHERE 
      `o`.`id_shop` = 1 
      AND `os`.`shipped` != 1 
      AND (
        `o`.`valid` = 1 
        OR (
          `os`.`id_order_state` != 28 
          AND `os`.`id_order_state` != 29
        )
      ) 
      AND `sa`.`id_product` = `od`.`product_id` 
      AND `sa`.`id_product_attribute` = `od`.`product_attribute_id` 
    GROUP BY 
      `od`.`product_id`, 
      `od`.`product_attribute_id`
  ) 
WHERE 
  `sa`.`id_shop` = 1
```

[10/21/2024 12:06 PM] instazx2
i pulled the sub-query out, and added the "ps_stock_available" table back in as another join, using the 2 conditions in the WHERE clause:
```sql
    SELECT 
      SUM(`od`.`product_quantity` - `od`.`product_quantity_refunded`)
    FROM 
      `ps_orders` `o` 
      INNER JOIN `ps_order_detail` `od` ON `od`.`id_order` = `o`.`id_order` 
      INNER JOIN `ps_order_state` `os` ON `os`.`id_order_state` = `o`.`current_state`
      INNER JOIN `ps_stock_available` `sa` ON (`sa`.`id_product` = `od`.`product_id` AND `sa`.`id_product_attribute` = `od`.`product_attribute_id` )
    WHERE 
      `o`.`id_shop` = 1 
      AND `os`.`shipped` != 1 
      AND (
        `o`.`valid` = 1 
        OR (
          `os`.`id_order_state` != 28 
          AND `os`.`id_order_state` != 29
        )
      )
    GROUP BY 
      `od`.`product_id`, 
      `od`.`product_attribute_id`
```

this let me finally get an EXPLAIN

```sql
SELECT 
  `a`.`id_cart`, 
  `a`.`date_add` AS `date_add`, 
  `CONCAT` (
    LEFT (`c`.`firstname`, ?), 
    ?, 
    `c`.`lastname`
  ) `customer`, 
  `a`.`id_cart` `total`, 
  `ca`.`name` `carrier`, 
  `o`.`id_order`, 
  IF (
    `IFNULL` (`o`.`id_order`, ?) = ?, 
    IF (
      `TIME_TO_SEC` (
        `TIMEDIFF` (?, `a`.`date_add`)
      ) > ?, 
      ...
    ), 
    `o`.`id_order`
  ) AS STATUS, 
  IF (`o`.`id_order`, ?,...) `badge_success`, 
  IF (`o`.`id_order`, ?,...) `badge_danger`, 
  IF (`co`.`id_guest`, ?,...) `id_guest`, 
  `shop`.`name` AS `shop_name` 
FROM 
  `ps_cart` `a` 
  LEFT JOIN `ps_customer` `c` ON (
    `c`.`id_customer` = `a`.`id_customer`
  ) 
  LEFT JOIN `ps_currency` `cu` ON (
    `cu`.`id_currency` = `a`.`id_currency`
  ) 
  LEFT JOIN `ps_carrier` `ca` ON (
    `ca`.`id_carrier` = `a`.`id_carrier`
  ) 
  LEFT JOIN `ps_orders` `o` ON (`o`.`id_cart` = `a`.`id_cart`) 
  LEFT JOIN (
    SELECT 
      `id_guest` 
    FROM 
      `ps_connections` 
    WHERE 
      `TIME_TO_SEC` (
        `TIMEDIFF` (?, `date_add`)
      ) < ? 
    LIMIT 
      ?
  ) AS `co` ON `co`.`id_guest` = `a`.`id_guest` 
  LEFT JOIN `ps_shop`
```

[10/21/2024 12:24 PM] instazx2
ok, from `actions.php`, we have:
```php
if ($values['action'] == 'updateAll') {
    require_once(dirname(__FILE__) . '/bundlesstocks.php');

    $bundlesstocks = new Bundlesstocks();

    $shops = Shop::getShops(false);
    foreach ($shops as $shop) {
        $bundlesstocks->updateAllPacks(null, $shop['id_shop']);
    }

    die("OK");
}
```

which calls "updateAllPacks", passing `null` for the product id, and your shop ID for the shop id parameter:
```php
    public function updateAllPacks($id_product = null, $id_shop = null)
    {
        $allConfiguredPackIds = [];
        // check for the Configurator Pack
        if (Module::isEnabled('swt_productconfiguratorusingquestionnaire')) {
            // check if the class is loaded
            $ConfiguredPackProductFilePath = dirname(__FILE__, 2) . '/swt_productconfiguratorusingquestionnaire/model/ConfiguredPackProduct.php';
            if (file_exists($ConfiguredPackProductFilePath) == false) {
                return;
            }

            if (class_exists('ConfiguredPackProduct') == false) {
                require_once $ConfiguredPackProductFilePath;
            }

            // check if the pack is belongs to the Configurator module
            $allConfiguredPackIds = ConfiguredPackProduct::getAllPackIds();
            if (is_array($allConfiguredPackIds) && !empty($allConfiguredPackIds)) {
                $allConfiguredPackIds = array_column($allConfiguredPackIds, 'id_pack_product');
            }
        }

        $query = "SELECT `id_product_pack` FROM `" . _DB_PREFIX_. "pack`";
        if ($id_product) {
            $query .= " WHERE id_product_pack = " . $id_product;
        }

        if ($results = Db::getInstance()->ExecuteS($query)) {
            foreach ($results as $row) {
                // skip the configured pack products
                if (in_array($row['id_product_pack'], $allConfiguredPackIds) == false) {
                    self::updatePackNew($row['id_product_pack'], $id_shop);
                }
            }
        }
    }
```


[10/21/2024 12:24 PM] instazx2

```php
    public static function getProductPhysicalQuantities(
        $id_product,
        $id_product_attribute,
        $id_shop,
        $id_warehouse = null
    ) {
        $query = 'SELECT s.id_warehouse, s.physical_quantity FROM `'._DB_PREFIX_.'stock` s LEFT JOIN `'._DB_PREFIX_.'warehouse_shop` ws ON (ws.`id_warehouse` = s.`id_warehouse`) WHERE `id_product` = "'.(int)$id_product.'"         }

        return Db::getInstance()->executeS($query);
    }
```

[10/21/2024 12:53 PM] instazx2
`updatePacks` is pretty simple:
```php
private function updatePacks($packs = array(), $id_shop = 1)
{
    if (!empty($packs)) {
        foreach ($packs as $pack) {
            self::updatePackNew($pack['id_product'], $id_shop);
        }
    }
}
```

at a high level, they're a way to combine a lot of logic & functionality into a single atomic operation.  rather than making hundreds or thousands of calls to the DB from the application layer, we can prepare a bundle of data that a stored procedure needs, pass it in, and let the thing run in one shot

[10/21/2024 1:21 PM] instazx2
i think we should have a stored procedure that (at a minimum) handles `hookActionUpdateQuantity`  we'd have a sproc that takes the 3 `$params` that are passed in for the hook, so instead of lines 245-309, we'd have a single call like:

```php
Db::getInstance()->executeS('EXEC up_updateProductQuantity', [
  'id_product' => $params['id_product'],
  'id_product_attribute' => $params['id_product_attribute'],
  'quantity' => $params['quantity'],
  'id_shop' => $this->context->shop->id,
  . . . (anything else needed) . . .
]);
```

inside the `up_updateProductQuantity` procedure, we bundle the calls into a single monolithic UPDATE-WITH-JOINS statement


[10/21/2024 1:23 PM] instazx2
the cronjob one, `updateAllPacks`, will probably be a single call to a two-stage sproc.  stage 1 is creating a temporary table and populating it with the `SELECT pack_id FROM ps_packs` query, then passing that temporary table to the same (shared?) code that `up_updateProductQuantity` is using

this can be implemented as a mysql user-defined FUNCTION, or maybe just handled as part of the query itself:
```php
getMinQuantityNew($items) {
    $min = $items[0]['quantity_in_stock']/$items[0]['quantity_in_pack'];
    foreach ($items as $item) {
        $min_to_check = $item['quantity_in_stock']/$item['quantity_in_pack'];
        if ($min_to_check < $min) {
            $min = $min_to_check;
        }
    }
    return floor($min);
}
```

[10/21/2024 2:15 PM] instazx2
```php
public static function getItems($id_product, $id_lang)
{
    if (!Pack::isFeatureActive()) {
        return [];
    }

    if (array_key_exists($id_product, self::$cachePackItems)) {
        return self::$cachePackItems[$id_product];
    }
    $result = Db::getInstance()->executeS('SELECT id_product_item, id_product_attribute_item, quantity FROM `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'pack` where id_product_pack = ' . (int) $id_product);
    $array_result = [];
    foreach ($result as $row) {
        $p = new Product($row['id_product_item'], false, $id_lang);
        $p->loadStockData();
        $p->pack_quantity = $row['quantity'];
        $p->id_pack_product_attribute = (isset($row['id_product_attribute_item']) && $row['id_product_attribute_item'] ? $row['id_product_attribute_item'] : 0);
        if (isset($row['id_product_attribute_item']) && $row['id_product_attribute_item']) {
            $sql = 'SELECT agl.`name` AS group_name, al.`name` AS attribute_name
                    FROM `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'product_attribute` pa
                    ' . Shop::addSqlAssociation('product_attribute', 'pa') . '
                    LEFT JOIN `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'product_attribute_combination` pac ON pac.`id_product_attribute` = pa.`id_product_attribute`
                    LEFT JOIN `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'attribute` a ON a.`id_attribute` = pac.`id_attribute`
                    LEFT JOIN `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'attribute_group` ag ON ag.`id_attribute_group` = a.`id_attribute_group`
                    LEFT JOIN `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'attribute_lang` al ON (a.`id_attribute` = al.`id_attribute` AND al.`id_lang` = ' . (int) Context::getContext()->language->id . ')
                    LEFT JOIN `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'attribute_group_lang` agl ON (ag.`id_attribute_group` = agl.`id_attribute_group` AND agl.`id_lang` = ' . (int) Context::getContext()->language->id . ')
                    WHERE pa.`id_product_attribute` = ' . $row['id_product_attribute_item'] . '
                    GROUP BY pa.`id_product_attribute`, ag.`id_attribute_group`
                    ORDER BY pa.`id_product_attribute`';

            $combinations = Db::getInstance()->executeS($sql);
            foreach ($combinations as $k => $combination) {
                $p->name .= ' ' . $combination['group_name'] . '-' . $combination['attribute_name'];
            }
        }
        $array_result[] = $p;
    }
    self::$cachePackItems[$id_product] = $array_result;

    return self::$cachePackItems[$id_product];
}
```

why does it run one query and then immediately loop over the result and run N more queries


[10/21/2024 2:18 PM] brett4091
Looks like maybe a lot of it is to do with "attributes", which is basically what a product variant is called in PrestaShop


[10/21/2024 2:19 PM] brett4091
Product A can have a single attribute assigned of "color" containing a bunch of different colors


[10/21/2024 2:27 PM] brett4091
So ultimately in a single product pack, you can have a dozen or more products, and each product can have its own attributes (or variants)


[10/21/2024 2:43 PM] instazx2
look at the second line inside the foreach:  `$p->loadStockData();`.  i went and found that in Product.php:
```php
public function loadStockData()
{
    if (false === Validate::isLoadedObject($this)) {
        return;
    }

    // Default product quantity is available quantity to sell in current shop
    $this->quantity = StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct($this->id, 0);   // <-- runs 1 query
    $this->out_of_stock = StockAvailable::outOfStock($this->id);                     // <-- runs 1 query
    $this->depends_on_stock = StockAvailable::dependsOnStock($this->id);             // <-- runs 1 query
    $this->location = StockAvailable::getLocation($this->id) ?: '';                  // <-- runs 1 query

    if (Context::getContext()->shop->getContext() == Shop::CONTEXT_GROUP && Context::getContext()->shop->getContextShopGroup()->share_stock == 1) {
        $this->advanced_stock_management = $this->useAdvancedStockManagement();
    }
}
```

then after that, it potentially runs that much larger SELECT statement with 5 joins ... for every single item in the pack (or vise-versa, can't quite tell yet) the calling code is running 4 separate queries *to the same table*, and the code that actually does this (inside the hook) doesn't even use all 4 of those extra fields that it loads 🤦.  all it uses are `pack_id` and `pack_product_attribute_id` -- it turns around and re-queries some of those fields AGAIN

{Reactions}
🤮

[10/21/2024 3:26 PM] instazx2
those 4 could be 1 query returning 4 columns from the same table:
```php
$this->quantity = StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct($this->id, 0);   // <-- runs 1 query
$this->out_of_stock = StockAvailable::outOfStock($this->id);                     // <-- runs 1 query
$this->depends_on_stock = StockAvailable::dependsOnStock($this->id);             // <-- runs 1 query
$this->location = StockAvailable::getLocation($this->id) ?: '';                  // <-- runs 1 query
```
could become
```php
$stock_params = StockAvailable::getStockParameters($this->id);
$this->quantity = $stock_params['quantity'];
$this->out_of_stock = $stock_params['out_of_stock'];
$this->depends_on_stock = $stock_params['depends_on_stock'];
$this->location = $stock_params['location'] ?: '';
```

assuming we then implemented the following method:
```php
public static function getStockParameters($id_product) {
    $query = new DbQuery();
    $query->select('quantity,out_of_stock,depends_on_stock,location');
    $query->from('stock_available');
    $query->where('id_product = ' . (int)$id_product);
    $query->where('id_product_attribute = 0');
    
    return Db::getInstance(_PS_USE_SQL_SLAVE_)->getRow($query);
}
```

```
    /**
     * Fill the variables used for stock management.
     */
    public function loadStockData()
    {
        if (false === Validate::isLoadedObject($this)) {
            return;
        }

        // Default product quantity is available quantity to sell in current shop
        $this->quantity = StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct($this->id, 0);
        $this->out_of_stock = StockAvailable::outOfStock($this->id);
        $this->location = StockAvailable::getLocation($this->id) ?: '';
    }
```

```php
    foreach ($itemsInPack as $pack) {
```

```php
$query = "SELECT `id_product_pack` FROM `"._DB_PREFIX_."pack` WHERE `id_product_item` = ".(int)$params['id_product']." AND `id_product_attribute_item` = ".(int)$params['id_product_attribute'];

    // check if the class is loaded
    $ConfiguredPackProductFilePath = dirname(__FILE__, 2) . '/swt_productconfiguratorusingquestionnaire/model/ConfiguredPackProduct.php';

        if (class_exists('ConfiguredPackProduct') == false) {
            require_once $ConfiguredPackProductFilePath;
        }

        // get all configured pack IDs
        $allConfiguredPackIds = ConfiguredPackProduct::getAllPackIds();

AND `id_product_attribute_item` = ".(int)$params['id_product_attribute']." AND `id_product_pack` NOT IN(". implode(',', array_column($allConfiguredPackIds, 'id_pack_product')) .") ";

if ($results = Db::getInstance()->ExecuteS($query)) {
```

[10/21/2024 5:01 PM] instazx2
i've rolled the multiple nested queries into a single more complicated query:


[10/21/2024 5:02 PM] instazx2
```sql
WITH matching_product_packs AS (
    SELECT id_product_pack FROM ps_pack p
    LEFT JOIN ps_swt_customer_configurator c ON p.id_product_pack = c.id_pack_product
    WHERE id_product_item = 143 
    AND id_product_attribute_item = 42
    AND c.id_pack_product IS NULL
)
SELECT p.id_product_item, p.id_product_attribute_item, SUM(a.quantity) AS quantity
FROM ps_pack p
INNER JOIN matching_product_packs k ON p.id_product_pack = k.id_product_pack
INNER JOIN ps_stock_available a ON p.id_product_item = a.id_product AND p.id_product_attribute_item = a.id_product_attribute
GROUP BY p.id_product_item, p.id_product_attribute_item
```

[10/21/2024 5:57 PM] instazx2
it's using it here:
```php
$id_stock_available = (int) StockAvailable::getStockAvailableIdByProductId($id_product, $id_product_attribute, $id_shop);
if ($id_stock_available) {
    $stock_available = new StockAvailable($id_stock_available);

    $deltaQuantity = -1 * ((int) $stock_available->quantity - (int) $quantity);

    $stock_available->quantity = (int) $quantity;
    $stock_available->update();

    if (true === $add_movement && 0 != $deltaQuantity) {
        $stockManager->saveMovement($id_product, $id_product_attribute, $deltaQuantity);
    }
}
```

so that's the part where it actually updates the ps_stock_available table, and id_stock_available is used to map to the correct row

[10/21/2024 6:05 PM] brett4091
```
In PrestaShop 1.7.6.8, the id_stock_available column in the ps_stock_available table is likely the primary key for that table. It's an identifier that uniquely identifies each row in the stock availability table.
The ps_stock_available table typically stores information about the quantity of products available in stock. Each row in this table would represent the stock status for a specific product or product combination (if the product has attributes like size or color).
While I can't be 100% certain without directly examining the database schema, based on PrestaShop's typical structure, the id_stock_available column would serve these purposes:

It's a unique identifier for each stock entry.
It's likely an auto-incrementing integer.
It's used to link stock availability information to other tables if necessary.
```

ok, so the way PS is doing all this seems to be with the "hook actionUpdateQuantity" thing.  ( @Brett - KB3D if you're able to find all the places that's called, that'd be awesome ❤️ )  that gets kicked off by an order (i think), and in the case of a pack is when all hell breaks loose.


[10/22/2024 8:51 AM] instazx2
when that hook gets called, one of the earliest checks in the hook is `if (Pack::isPack($params['id_product']))` -- so, "is the quantity changing for a pack or individual product?"


[10/22/2024 8:52 AM] instazx2
if it's a pack, the code pulls all the products for the pack.  it then loops through all those products, and pulls all the related packs.  it then loops *again* through all the packs, and pulls all the products a second time


[10/22/2024 8:52 AM] instazx2
presumably before this happens, the stock quantity has been updated


[10/22/2024 8:53 AM] instazx2
it uses allllllllll those second products to calculate the number of packs in stock based on the lowest quantity of available products in the pack


[10/22/2024 8:54 AM] instazx2
as it's going through, whenever a pack quantity gets updated, it will kick off a recursive `actionUpdateQuantity` hook as well, starting the whole process again


[10/22/2024 8:55 AM] kbreezy

[10/22/2024 9:13 AM] instazx2
bundlesstocks.php hits that `isPack` branch, gets products, gets packs for those products, gets products for all those packs (each "get" for some reason takes like 4-12 queries to the database, so that *explodes*).  once it has *all* the products for any pack that's remotely involved, it does the `pack_quantity / item_quantity` math to determine the stock level of a pack, and then loops over those and calls `StockAvailable::setQuantity` for each remotely-involved pack with the newly-calculated stock level for that pack


[10/22/2024 9:14 AM] instazx2
inside `StockAvailable::setQuantity` is where it calls the stock movement code

[10/22/2024 9:55 AM] instazx2
this is my query so far -- it doesn't update anything yet
```sql
WITH product_stock AS (
    SELECT 
        p.id_product_pack,
        p.id_product_item, 
        p.id_product_attribute_item, 
        CAST(p.quantity AS REAL) AS pack_quantity, 
        CAST(SUM(a.quantity) AS REAL) AS item_quantity
    FROM ps_pack p
    LEFT JOIN ps_swt_customer_configurator c ON p.id_product_pack = c.id_pack_product
    INNER JOIN ps_stock_available a ON p.id_product_item = a.id_product AND p.id_product_attribute_item = a.id_product_attribute
    WHERE c.id_pack_product IS NULL
    GROUP BY
        p.id_product_pack,
        p.id_product_item, 
        p.id_product_attribute_item, 
        p.quantity
),
pack_quantities AS (
    SELECT 
        id_product_pack,    
        FLOOR(MIN(item_quantity / pack_quantity)) AS min_pack_quantity
    FROM product_stock
    GROUP BY 
        id_product_pack
)
SELECT 
    id_product_pack, 
    min_pack_quantity,
    s.quantity,
    (min_pack_quantity - s.quantity) AS delta
FROM pack_quantities q
INNER JOIN ps_stock_available s ON q.id_product_pack = s.id_product
WHERE s.id_product_attribute = 0
  AND (min_pack_quantity - s.quantity) <> 0
```

[10/22/2024 9:56 AM] instazx2

1) update the pack quantities
2) return the deltas so i can loop over them and fire off calls to "stock movement"


[10/22/2024 9:57 AM] instazx2

[10/22/2024 9:58 AM] instazx2
like what the hell is StockManager trying to do here?  why is it calculating deltas instead of just absolute numbers?
```php
public function updatePackQuantity($product, $stock_available, $delta_quantity, $id_shop = null)
{
    /** @TODO We should call the needed classes with the Symfony dependency injection instead of the Homemade Service Locator */
    $serviceLocator = new ServiceLocator();
    $configuration = $serviceLocator::get('\\PrestaShop\\PrestaShop\\Core\\ConfigurationInterface');

    if ($product->pack_stock_type == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_PRODUCTS_ONLY
        || $product->pack_stock_type == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_PACK_BOTH
        || ($product->pack_stock_type == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_DEFAULT
            && $configuration->get('PS_PACK_STOCK_TYPE') > 0)
    ) {
        $packItemsManager = $serviceLocator::get('\\PrestaShop\\PrestaShop\\Adapter\\Product\\PackItemsManager');
        $stockManager = $serviceLocator::get('\\PrestaShop\\PrestaShop\\Adapter\\StockManager');
        $cacheManager = $serviceLocator::get('\\PrestaShop\\PrestaShop\\Adapter\\CacheManager');

        $products_pack = $packItemsManager->getPackItems($product);
        foreach ($products_pack as $product_pack) {
            $productStockAvailable = $stockManager->getStockAvailableByProduct($product_pack, $product_pack->id_pack_product_attribute, $id_shop);
            $productStockAvailable->quantity = $productStockAvailable->quantity + ($delta_quantity * $product_pack->pack_quantity);
            $productStockAvailable->update();

            $cacheManager->clean('StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_' . (int) $product_pack->id . '*');
        }
    }

    $stock_available->quantity = $stock_available->quantity + $delta_quantity;

    if ($product->pack_stock_type == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_PACK_ONLY
        || $product->pack_stock_type == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_PACK_BOTH
        || (
            $product->pack_stock_type == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_DEFAULT
            && ($configuration->get('PS_PACK_STOCK_TYPE') == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_PACK_ONLY
                || $configuration->get('PS_PACK_STOCK_TYPE') == Pack::STOCK_TYPE_PACK_BOTH)
        )
    ) {
        $stock_available->update();
    }
}
```

[10/22/2024 10:15 AM] instazx2
at the bottom of `updateAllPacks`, there's this code:

```php
foreach ($results as $row) {
    // skip the configured pack products
    if (in_array($row['id_product_pack'], $allConfiguredPackIds) == false) {
        self::updatePackNew($row['id_product_pack'], $id_shop);
```

at the end of the non-pack branch of `hookActionUpdateQuantity` there's also this code:

which looks suspiciously similar to the method next to it:
```php
private function updatePacks($packs = array(), $id_shop = 1)
{
    if (!empty($packs)) {
        foreach ($packs as $pack) {
            self::updatePackNew($pack['id_product'], $id_shop);
        }
    }
}
```

[10/22/2024 11:37 AM] instazx2 (pinned)
well, anyway, my plan is:

* consolidate the couple places that the various "updatePack" methods are being called in bundlesstores.php to a single spot.  
* that single spot will take `$id_product` and `$id_product_attribute`, accepting `null` for either.
* call a new stored procedure, passing those two values into it
* inside the stored procedure:
* * create a temporary table with the following columns:  `id_product_pack, min_pack_quantity, existing_pack_quantity, delta`
* * pull the packs / products, filtering to `$id_product / $id_product_attribute` as needed
* * calculate the updated `min_pack_quantity` value, comparing it to the `existing_pack_quantity` value, recording that as `delta`   ( @Kbreezy - KB3D  the SQL i pasted above is already doing this)
* * insert any rows with a non-zero delta to the temporary table
* * join this temporary table back against `ps_stock_available`, and use the `min_pack_quantity` value to update the "quantity" field in the stock_available table
* * select all the rows back out of the temporary table
* back inside PHP, get all the rows that were selected back out
* loop over those rows, and call `StockManager::saveMovement` to record the changes in the packs

[10/22/2024 1:45 PM] instazx2
quick SQL review?  :hiding:   maybe @Brett - KB3D can throw it into claude or something?

```sql
DELIMITER ;;

DROP PROCEDURE IF EXISTS up_sync_pack_quantities;;
CREATE PROCEDURE up_sync_pack_quantities (
    IN p_id_product INT
)
BEGIN
    -- use a temporary table because CTE's are ephemeral and cross-talk between
    -- connections will be a major problem
    CREATE TEMPORARY TABLE IF NOT EXISTS t_pack_deltas AS
    WITH product_stock AS (
        SELECT 
            p.id_product_pack,
            p.id_product_item, 
            p.id_product_attribute_item, 
            CAST(p.quantity AS REAL) AS pack_quantity, 
            CAST(SUM(a.quantity) AS REAL) AS item_quantity
        FROM ps_pack p
        LEFT JOIN ps_swt_customer_configurator c ON p.id_product_pack = c.id_pack_product
        INNER JOIN ps_stock_available a ON p.id_product_item = a.id_product AND p.id_product_attribute_item = a.id_product_attribute
        WHERE c.id_pack_product IS NULL 
          AND (p_id_product IS NULL OR p.id_product_pack = p_id_product)
        GROUP BY
            p.id_product_pack,
            p.id_product_item, 
            p.id_product_attribute_item, 
            p.quantity
    ),
    pack_quantities AS (
        SELECT 
            id_product_pack,    
            FLOOR(MIN(item_quantity / pack_quantity)) AS min_pack_quantity
        FROM product_stock
        GROUP BY 
            id_product_pack
    )
    -- actually insert into the temp table here
    SELECT 
        id_product_pack, 
        min_pack_quantity,
        s.quantity AS existing_quantity,
        min_pack_quantity - s.quantity AS delta
    FROM pack_quantities q
    INNER JOIN ps_stock_available s ON q.id_product_pack = s.id_product AND s.id_product_attribute = 0
    WHERE min_pack_quantity - s.quantity <> 0;
    
    -- update the actual stock numbers if there are any values in the temp table
    UPDATE ps_stock_available s
    INNER JOIN t_pack_deltas d ON s.id_product = d.id_product_pack AND s.id_product_attribute = 0
    SET s.quantity = d.min_pack_quantity;
    
    -- return the relevant information so StockManager::saveMovement works
    SELECT id_product_pack, delta
    FROM t_pack_deltas;
    
    -- remove the temporary table explicitly
    DROP TABLE t_pack_deltas;
END;;
```


[10/22/2024 1:47 PM] brett4091
```
Let me review this SQL stored procedure for any potential issues.
The procedure looks generally well-structured, but there are a few points to consider:

Syntax and Structure:

The DELIMITER statement looks correct
The procedure parameters and variable declarations are properly formatted
The temporary table creation and cleanup logic is sound


Potential Issues:

In the product_stock CTE, the LEFT JOIN with ps_swt_customer_configurator followed by WHERE c.id_pack_product IS NULL effectively makes it an ANTI JOIN. Consider using NOT EXISTS instead for potentially better performance
The CAST AS REAL might not be necessary depending on your database system - if these columns are already numeric types
The temporary table creation uses IF NOT EXISTS, but there's no cleanup before the procedure runs - this could cause issues if a previous execution failed


Suggested Improvements:
```

[10/22/2024 3:20 PM] instazx2
@Kbreezy - KB3D this is it 🙂

```php
    private static function bulkUpdatePacks() {
        self::log("bulkUpdatePacks: handling all pack counts");

        // call stored procedure and get the result set back
        $changes = Db::getInstance()->executeS("CALL up_sync_pack_quantities(NULL);");
        if (!$changes) {
            self::log("bulkUpdatePacks: no stock quantity changes were detected");
            return;
        }

        $stockManager = ServiceLocator::get('\\PrestaShop\\PrestaShop\\Core\\Stock\\StockManager');
        Context::getContext()->employee = self::getFirstEmployee();

        foreach($changes as $change) {

            $id_product = (int) $change['id_product_pack'];
            $delta = (int) $change['delta'];

            Cache::clean('StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_' . $id_product . '*');
            $stockManager->saveMovement($id_product, 0, $delta);
        }


        self::log("bulkUpdatePacks: " . count($changes) . " packs were updated");        
    }
```

[10/22/2024 3:25 PM] instazx2
remember how we were confused why it didn't blow up in infinite recursion?  that's here:

```php
public function hookActionUpdateQuantity($params)
{
    if (!Configuration::get("BUNDLESSTOCKS_AUTO")) {
        return;
    }
    
    $id_shop = isset($params['id_shop']) ? (int)$params['id_shop'] : (int)$this->context->shop->id;
    $id_product = (int)$params['id_product'];
    $id_product_attribute = (int)$params['id_product_attribute'];

    // see if we've already handled this shop/product/attribute, return early if so
    if (isset(self::$qty_update_hook_called[$id_shop][$id_product][$id_product_attribute])) {
        return;
    }
    
    // set that we've handled this shop/product/attribute so when the hook fires again, we don't trigger here
    self::$qty_update_hook_called[$id_shop][$id_product][$id_product_attribute] = true;
    self::log("hookActionUpdateQuantity for Product #".$id_product.' (attribute #'.$id_product_attribute.')');        
    self::bulkUpdatePacks();
}
```

[10/23/2024 1:43 PM] instazx2
no, a db change will not fire the hook.  that's why the hooks exist -- one thing changes the DB and then the other code has to be triggered via that hook to do other things


[10/23/2024 1:44 PM] instazx2
in the normal PS code it's like
```php
  $stock->update(id: 2005, qty: 3);
  run_hook('actionUpdatePacks', { "id" : 2005 });
```


[10/23/2024 1:45 PM] instazx2
so when the site itself is updating stock levels, the instant the database call is done it'll turn around and fire the hook that updates the associated packs


[10/23/2024 1:45 PM] instazx2
which means we can't use the site itself to get the inventory into the half-correct condition to manually run through the code

[10/23/2024 4:28 PM] instazx2
the ugly moved to the sproc
```sql
DELIMITER ;;

DROP PROCEDURE IF EXISTS up_sync_pack_quantities;;
CREATE PROCEDURE up_sync_pack_quantities ()
BEGIN
    DECLARE EXIT HANDLER FOR SQLEXCEPTION
    BEGIN
        -- Cleanup on error
        DROP TEMPORARY TABLE IF EXISTS t_pack_deltas;
        RESIGNAL;
    END;

    -- use a temporary table because CTE's are ephemeral and cross-talk between
    -- connections will be a major problem
    DROP TEMPORARY TABLE IF EXISTS t_pack_deltas;
    CREATE TEMPORARY TABLE t_pack_deltas AS
    WITH product_stock AS (
        SELECT 
            p.id_product_pack,
            p.id_product_item, 
            p.id_product_attribute_item, 
            CAST(p.quantity AS REAL) AS pack_quantity, 
            CAST(SUM(a.quantity) AS REAL) AS item_quantity
        FROM ps_pack p
        LEFT JOIN ps_swt_customer_configurator c ON p.id_product_pack = c.id_pack_product
        INNER JOIN ps_stock_available a ON p.id_product_item = a.id_product AND p.id_product_attribute_item = a.id_product_attribute
        WHERE c.id_pack_product IS NULL
        GROUP BY
            p.id_product_pack,
            p.id_product_item, 
            p.id_product_attribute_item, 
            p.quantity
    ),
    pack_quantities AS (
        SELECT 
            id_product_pack,    
            FLOOR(MIN(item_quantity / pack_quantity)) AS min_pack_quantity
        FROM product_stock
        GROUP BY 
            id_product_pack
    )
    -- actually insert into the temp table here
    SELECT 
        id_product_pack, 
        min_pack_quantity,
        s.quantity AS existing_quantity,
        min_pack_quantity - s.quantity AS delta
    FROM pack_quantities q
    INNER JOIN ps_stock_available s ON q.id_product_pack = s.id_product AND s.id_product_attribute = 0
    WHERE min_pack_quantity - s.quantity <> 0;
    
    -- update the actual stock numbers if there are any values in the temp table
    UPDATE ps_stock_available s
    INNER JOIN t_pack_deltas d ON s.id_product = d.id_product_pack AND s.id_product_attribute = 0
    SET s.quantity = d.min_pack_quantity;
    
    -- insert the relevant information into ps_stock_mvt
    INSERT INTO ps_stock_mvt 
        (id_stock, id_stock_mvt_reason, id_employee, employee_lastname, employee_firstname, physical_quantity, date_add, sign)
    SELECT
        s.id_stock_available,
        IF(d.delta > 0, 11, 12),
        1,
        'Brickman',
        'Kris',
        ABS(d.delta),
        CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(),
        IF(d.delta > 0, 1, -1)
    FROM t_pack_deltas d
    INNER JOIN ps_stock_available s ON d.id_product_pack = s.id_product AND s.id_product_attribute = 0;
        
END;;
```

[10/23/2024 4:42 PM] instazx2
inside of StockAvailable.php:
```php
    public static function getQuantityAvailableByProduct($id_product = null, $id_product_attribute = null, $id_shop = null)
    {
        // if null, it's a product without attributes
        if ($id_product_attribute === null) {
            $id_product_attribute = 0;
        }

        $key = 'StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_' . (int) $id_product . '-' . (int) $id_product_attribute . '-' . (int) $id_shop;
        if (!Cache::isStored($key)) {
            $query = new DbQuery();
            $query->select('SUM(quantity)');
            $query->from('stock_available');

            // if null, it's a product without attributes
            if ($id_product !== null) {
                $query->where('id_product = ' . (int) $id_product);
            }

            $query->where('id_product_attribute = ' . (int) $id_product_attribute);
            $query = StockAvailable::addSqlShopRestriction($query, $id_shop);
            $result = (int) Db::getInstance(_PS_USE_SQL_SLAVE_)->getValue($query);
            Cache::store($key, $result);

            return $result;
        }

        return Cache::retrieve($key);
    }
```


[10/23/2024 4:42 PM] instazx2
towards the top is the `Cache::isStored` call, looking at that key


[10/23/2024 4:43 PM] instazx2
quick & dirtiest way is to stick a `Cache::clear('StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_*');` right after the DB call


[10/23/2024 4:43 PM] instazx2
```php
    private static function bulkUpdatePacks() {
        self::log("bulkUpdatePacks: handling all pack counts");

        Db::getInstance()->executeS("CALL up_sync_pack_quantities();");
        Cache::clear('StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_*');
    }
```


[10/23/2024 4:44 PM] instazx2

[10/23/2024 4:54 PM] brett4091
lol... yeah, I think quick and dirty is fine


[10/23/2024 4:55 PM] instazx2
then pls to be updating the `bulkUpdatePacks()` method in the file i sent above with this variant instead 🙂


[10/23/2024 4:55 PM] brett4091
I didn't know if we were talking ms or minutes


[10/23/2024 4:56 PM] instazx2
fair enough, just as a standard point though, i won't suggest things that will add more than ~ 250ms to the end user experience in the worst case


[10/23/2024 4:57 PM] instazx2
and it better be a REAL damn good value-add to justify 250ms


[10/23/2024 4:58 PM] brett4091

[10/23/2024 4:59 PM] instazx2
well so far i have about, what, -6000ms of goodwill i can fill back up?


[10/23/2024 4:59 PM] brett4091
That's on the backend and checkout, but absolutely! Whatcha wanna put back into the void?


[10/23/2024 5:00 PM] instazx2
the problem with the quick & dirty method is it blows away *all* products, so people looking at *any* product on the site (pack or not) will incur that 0.1 - 1.0ms latency on the first load.  it'll be gone after that, until the next purchase goes through


[10/23/2024 5:00 PM] instazx2
and it's not per-user, it's per-product.  so if a bot is crawling the site, the bot may inadvertently end up repopulating the cache for us


[10/23/2024 5:02 PM] brett4091
I assume we can't trigger the caching process ourselves from the module code?


[10/23/2024 5:02 PM] instazx2
clearing the cache on a per-product basis would mean that only the stock-affected items would need to be repopulated, but i also don't know the full scope of what's happening in the cache.  for the hook, is it just the packs that i need to worry about?  is it the packs and products in the packs?  is it the packs, products, and then second-order packs?


[10/23/2024 5:02 PM] instazx2
we can, but that happens within the user's request, so they'd have to wait for it


[10/23/2024 5:03 PM] instazx2
i'm kinda dumb, i can run the same query the cache-populate code is as part of my sproc and *update* the cache when it's done


[10/23/2024 5:03 PM] instazx2
rather than clearing and waiting


[10/23/2024 5:03 PM] brett4091
What if we registered a custom hook, called that instead, and then handled it in another function (or wost case a different module)


[10/23/2024 5:04 PM] brett4091
Ahh nice, that would probably be ideal


[10/23/2024 5:04 PM] brett4091
Not that the 0.1 - 1 increase occasionally would be the end of the world, but it just seemed weirdly inefficient considering all the other improvements


[10/23/2024 5:07 PM] instazx2
it annoyed me that it's there, when the whole reason i was proposing it was "i'm too lazy to figure out how to clear the relevant parts of the cache"


[10/23/2024 5:08 PM] brett4091
```
Cache::clear('StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_*');
```


[10/23/2024 5:08 PM] brett4091
The parameter is a prefix, correct?


[10/23/2024 5:09 PM] brett4091
I haven't looked in the DB yet but seems like the trailing part of that key would be the product IDs


[10/23/2024 5:09 PM] instazx2
yeah, the full key is `StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_{productId}_{attributeId}_{storeId}`


[10/23/2024 5:09 PM] instazx2
as soon as you put an asterik in, everything after it gets included


[10/23/2024 5:11 PM] instazx2
so the line with just `...Product_*` will blow away the cache of all products, all attributes, all stores.  if it's `...Product_2006_*`, that'll be just product 2006, but all attributes across all stores


[10/23/2024 5:11 PM] brett4091
and we know what products we're dealing with in the module, so we could probably do something along the lines of 
```
Cache::clear('StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_productId_*');
```
Even if we have to create a global array in the module to keep track of those between functions


[10/23/2024 5:11 PM] brett4091
Or something of that nature, just spitballing and may be entirely wrong lol


[10/23/2024 5:13 PM] instazx2
we know what product we're dealing with in the hook, because it's passed in, but it may not be the final thing that needs to be cleared.  i just added this to the end of the sproc,
```sql
    -- return product cache information
    SELECT 
        d.id_product_pack, 
        SUM(s.quantity) AS quantity
    FROM t_pack_deltas d
    INNER JOIN ps_stock_available s ON d.id_product_pack = s.id_product AND s.id_product_attribute = 0
    GROUP BY d.id_product_pack;
```


[10/23/2024 5:14 PM] instazx2
so it'll send back any pack that got updated and the new, correct quantity.  i'll update bundlesstocks.php to just set the cache value of `StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_{productId}_0_1` to whatever the 'quantity' value is


[10/23/2024 5:15 PM] brett4091
Brilliant!


[10/23/2024 5:18 PM] instazx2
this gonna blow up if breezy adds another shop though


[10/23/2024 5:18 PM] brett4091
Haha, I don't see that happening


[10/23/2024 5:19 PM] instazx2
ok, update your bundlesstocks.php file with:
```php
    private static function bulkUpdatePacks() {
        self::log("bulkUpdatePacks: handling all pack counts");

        $updates = Db::getInstance()->executeS("CALL up_sync_pack_quantities();");
        foreach($updates as $update) {
            $key = 'StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_' . (int) $update['id_product_pack'] . '_0_1';
            $value = (int) $update['quantity'];

            Cache::set($key, $value);
        }
    }
```

https://images-ext-1.discordapp.net/external/1003x7HVrcJxWzE0Es3wPI-WIrlnjvZ7Qi0Wese0ulw/https/media.tenor.com/RxyGFmmfWsQAAAAe/amen-brother-kid.png


[10/23/2024 7:34 PM] instazx2
after my stored procedure runs, what's in the cache doesn't match what's in the database.  the original code handled that by clearing the cache per-product as it updated the packs, but all that code is so complex to follow it's hard to tell if it's clearing just the pack quantities or packs+products+secondary packs.


[10/23/2024 7:35 PM] instazx2
the easiest and most effective thing is to blow away the whole product cache, because there's no way we'll miss updating one.  the product cache is per-product/attribute, so any time a page needs to show the in-stock quantity of something it first checks the cache and will hit the database if it's missing


[10/23/2024 7:37 PM] instazx2
the cache is site-wide, not per-user.  so when any user (guest, logged in, admin, whoever) does anything that triggers the pack-update hook, the site-wide cache would be cleared and have to get rebuilt as the products are looked at again.  repopulating the cache isn't that slow, there's just a lot of things to populate.  seems like a relatively persistent whack-a-mole trying to repopulate them as they're constantly cleared


[10/23/2024 7:37 PM] instazx2
the other side of it is that if we don't clear enough of the cache, users will see incorrect stock quantities of products, which is why i was leaning towards being more aggressive than less


[10/23/2024 7:38 PM] instazx2
i would hope that PS ultimately catches the quantity errors before the checkout process finishes, and it's just a minor annoyance until we realize the cache-clearing isn't aggressive enough.  if it relies on the value in the cache to determine if an order can go through, that's a bigger problem beyond just this particular thing


[10/23/2024 7:41 PM] kbreezy
Understood. Not so understood is this product cache you're speaking to. To my knowledge aside from CSS cacheing we have no such thing implemented yet. Does prestashop naturally cache things? Or is this a sql built-in system for performance, like a cpu or ssd would have?


[10/23/2024 7:42 PM] instazx2

[10/23/2024 7:43 PM] instazx2
that would be the kind of thing Redis is used for, if you've seen that in the more advanced configurations

my guess is that something generates that file on the fly and then includes it in the page as it's being rendered.  since the captchas have a secret key server-side to track the success/failure, that appears to be what's written into the file (the first line of the `array` call).  because that value is different for every captcha, by design, that means the smarty cache will never match an existing file because each one is different

[10/24/2024 2:31 PM] brett4091
```
While I aim to help, I should note that I can't be completely certain about undocumented PrestaShop features from 2020. However, in PrestaShop 1.7.6.8, typically:
The top Smarty cache option controls template caching - it caches the compiled Smarty template files to improve page rendering performance.
The bottom "cache" option is likely the system/general cache setting that controls caching of database queries, configuration files, and other application data. This helps reduce database load and speeds up common operations by storing frequently accessed data in memory/cache.
```

[10/24/2024 3:50 PM] instazx2
@Brett - KB3D `getPriceStatic` is fucking bonkers:
```php
if (!$context) {
            $context = Context::getContext();
        }

        $cur_cart = $context->cart;

        if ($divisor !== null) {
            Tools::displayParameterAsDeprecated('divisor');
        }

        if (!Validate::isBool($usetax) || !Validate::isUnsignedId($id_product)) {
            die(Tools::displayError());
        }

        // Initializations
        $id_group = null;
        if ($id_customer) {
            $id_group = Customer::getDefaultGroupId((int) $id_customer);
        }
        if (!$id_group) {
            $id_group = (int) Group::getCurrent()->id;
        }

        // If there is cart in context or if the specified id_cart is different from the context cart id
        if (!is_object($cur_cart) || (Validate::isUnsignedInt($id_cart) && $id_cart && $cur_cart->id != $id_cart)) {
            if (!$id_cart && !isset($context->employee)) {
                die(Tools::displayError());
            }
            $cur_cart = new Cart($id_cart);
            // Store cart in context to avoid multiple instantiations in BO
            if (!Validate::isLoadedObject($context->cart)) {
                $context->cart = $cur_cart;
            }
        }

        $cart_quantity = 0;
        if ((int) $id_cart) {
            $cache_id = 'Product::getPriceStatic_' . (int) $id_product . '-' . (int) $id_cart;
            if (!Cache::isStored($cache_id) || ($cart_quantity = Cache::retrieve($cache_id) != (int) $quantity)) {
                $sql = 'SELECT SUM(`quantity`)
                FROM `' . _DB_PREFIX_ . 'cart_product`
                WHERE `id_product` = ' . (int) $id_product . '
                AND `id_cart` = ' . (int) $id_cart;
                $cart_quantity = (int) Db::getInstance(_PS_USE_SQL_SLAVE_)->getValue($sql);
                Cache::store($cache_id, $cart_quantity);
            } else {
                $cart_quantity = Cache::retrieve($cache_id);
            }
        }

        $id_currency = Validate::isLoadedObject($context->currency) ? (int) $context->currency->id : (int) Configuration::get('PS_CURRENCY_DEFAULT');

        if (!$id_address && Validate::isLoadedObject($cur_cart)) {
            $id_address = $cur_cart->{Configuration::get('PS_TAX_ADDRESS_TYPE')};
        }

// retrieve address informations
        $address = Address::initialize($id_address, true);
        $id_country = (int) $address->id_country;
        $id_state = (int) $address->id_state;
        $zipcode = $address->postcode;

        if (Tax::excludeTaxeOption()) {
            $usetax = false;
        }

        if ($usetax != false
            && !empty($address->vat_number)
            && $address->id_country != Configuration::get('VATNUMBER_COUNTRY')
            && Configuration::get('VATNUMBER_MANAGEMENT')) {
            $usetax = false;
        }

        if (null === $id_customer && Validate::isLoadedObject($context->customer)) {
            $id_customer = $context->customer->id;
        }

        $return = Product::priceCalculation(
            $context->shop->id,
            $id_product,
            $id_product_attribute,
            $id_country,
            $id_state,
            $zipcode,
            $id_currency,
            $id_group,
            $quantity,
            $usetax,
            $decimals,
            $only_reduc,
            $usereduc,
            $with_ecotax,
            $specific_price_output,
            $use_group_reduction,
            $id_customer,
            $use_customer_price,
            $id_cart,
            $cart_quantity,
            $id_customization
        );

        return $return;
```

[10/25/2024 1:32 PM] instazx2
media server would very likely help pagespeed for a few reasons:
1) it's a different domain, so the browers will be able to parallelize connections
2) the media server doesn't have to run PHP, so it's just static file serving -- removing the overhead there
3) i don't have to check in hundreds of binary files to Git


[10/25/2024 1:33 PM] brett4091
I feel like #3 is the real motivation 😄

[10/29/2024 9:23 AM] instazx2
i didn't do *too* much to it, it was the first iteration of the common-rail injection.  the Mk4s could get to stupid power

mine was a stick, and factory they're like 105hp @ 180tq.  i got a chiptune, which is a fucking cheat code on a diesel -- *just* a flash brought me to 135hp @ 265tq ... at like 1800rpm.  i replaced all the speakers with passive-crossover components, ran everything to a 4x100 amp, new head unit with low-level outputs.  added a second 1x400 amp for a 12" sub in a ported box.  dynamatted the whole car -- doors, floors, entire trunk.  upgraded alternator, added a skidplate.  the traction control system was unreal, i could hill-start on packed slush without spinning the tires -- which meant it was a monster off road as well.

i added a class 1 hitch.  it towed a double-axle car trailer with the aforementioned ZX2 on it from KC to Indiana, got 38mpg at 70mph (unladen got 50mpg pretty regularly).  road trips would routinely crest 650 miles of range, and i hit 700 once.

it comfortably held 5 people, it comfortably could cruise at 115mph on the highway while feeling like 70, that much torque down low meant i could put that car anywhere i wanted to around town beating anyone off the line.  

the upgraded alternator and speakers meant that when the rednecks at a campout got into a fight and the DJ took all his gear because the other redneck fucked his sister, i popped the doors and trunk open and DJ'ed instead from 45 feet away, with perfectly clear sound and zero distortion.

{Reactions}

[11/8/2024 1:41 PM] instazx2
this is effectively what bundlesstocks.php is now.  there's still the unrelated code for installing & configuring the module at the top, but most of the configuration is now ignored.

```php
public function hookActionUpdateQuantity($params)
{
    if (!Configuration::get("BUNDLESSTOCKS_AUTO")) {
        return;
    }
    
    $id_shop = isset($params['id_shop']) ? (int)$params['id_shop'] : (int)$this->context->shop->id;
    $id_product = (int)$params['id_product'];
    $id_product_attribute = (int)$params['id_product_attribute'];

    if (isset(self::$qty_update_hook_called[$id_shop][$id_product][$id_product_attribute])) {
        return;
    }
    
    self::$qty_update_hook_called[$id_shop][$id_product][$id_product_attribute] = true;
    self::log("hookActionUpdateQuantity for Product #".$id_product.' (attribute #'.$id_product_attribute.')');        
    self::bulkUpdatePacks();
}

// this is called by the cron script, although it's mostly unnecessary at this point
public function updateAllPacks($id_product = null, $id_shop = null)
{
    self::bulkUpdatePacks();
}

private static function bulkUpdatePacks() {
    self::log("bulkUpdatePacks: handling all pack counts");

    $updates = Db::getInstance()->executeS("CALL up_sync_pack_quantities();");
    foreach($updates as $update) {
        $key = 'StockAvailable::getQuantityAvailableByProduct_' . (int) $update['id_product_pack'] . '_0_1';
        $value = (int) $update['quantity'];

        Cache::set($key, $value);
    }
}
```

so anything that calls `hook("actionUpdateQuantity");` with any params will update the whole pack inventory (ignoring the params).  the `updateAllPacks` method was/is called by that cron job.  both of them funnel into the private `bulkUpdatePacks()` method which is where the sproc is actually called

[11/11/2024 9:24 AM] kbreezy
Soo here's the order of events...

Kris introduces a local pickup option during checkout so that customers don't have to use a promo code

People near and far (international even) start selecting local pickup (free) expecting to be able to drive to their local walmart and pick up their goods

Kris decides to only offer this option to customers with shipping addresses in Ohio to save his sanity

Kris creates a custom shipping rule that does this by only showing certain carriers (all but local pick up) to customers outside of Ohio

Kris updates the UPS module, which (because presta developers are genius) removes all old carrier ID's and reinstalls them with new.

Kris is smart enough to update custom shipping rules with the new carrier ID's, and even double checks them 10 times

Kris is *not* smart enough to catch the local pickup filter.

And thus any new carrier ID's that should be shown to residents outside of Ohio were omitted (AKA all new UPS carriers)


The thing that finally tipped me off is that my own address would routinely return rates, along with international addresses. And the Chris2 said it worked for him too (he's in Ohio). But that wasn't enough for my pea brain. The module developer came back to our support request showing everything working fine for the address they tried (Sandusky Ohio)

**why** i'm wanting to write a new store backend:
* kris takes care of me and i want to return the favor as i can
* nobody has been happy with any existing solutions, and by far the best strategy is add another option to the mix
* i'm working on a library for high-performance "distributed, event-sourced, asynchronous, cloud-native, online transaction processing", and a storefront seemed like a good fit for a tech demo

**how** i'm wanting to write a new store backend:
* C#, .NET 10, MassTransit, [some db], RabbitMq, and Docker
* RESTful API pushing almost all of the rendering work to a javascript application on the frontend
* using published events in a messagebus on the backend for event hooks
* forwarding events via SignalR to the frontend

so @Brett - KB3D  how many of those words do you understand and where should i start

[7/23/2025 12:34 PM] instazx2
this mf'er, i'm wondering if we could use a different approach as well

```
SELECT DISTINCT 
  p.id_product, pl.name, pl.description_short, p.price,
  cl.name as category_name, m.name as manufacturer_name,
  sa.quantity, i.id_image, pl.link_rewrite,
  MATCH(pl.name, pl.description_short) AGAINST("' . $ft_search_term . '" IN BOOLEAN MODE) as relevance_score,
  w.calculated_weight, w.manual_boost
FROM `ps_product` p
LEFT JOIN `ps_product_lang` pl ON (p.id_product = pl.id_product AND pl.id_lang = ' . (int)$id_lang . ')
LEFT JOIN `ps_category_lang` cl ON (p.id_category_default = cl.id_category AND cl.id_lang = ' . (int)$id_lang . ')
LEFT JOIN `ps_manufacturer` m ON (p.id_manufacturer = m.id_manufacturer)
LEFT JOIN `ps_stock_available` sa ON (p.id_product = sa.id_product AND sa.id_product_attribute = 0 AND sa.id_shop = ' . (int)$id_shop . ')
LEFT JOIN `ps_image` i ON (p.id_product = i.id_product AND i.cover = 1)
LEFT JOIN `ps_product_shop` ps ON (p.id_product = ps.id_product AND ps.id_shop = ' . (int)$id_shop . ')
LEFT JOIN `ps_category` c ON (p.id_category_default = c.id_category)
LEFT JOIN `ps_prestosearch_weights` w ON (p.id_product = w.product_id)
WHERE p.active = 1 
AND ps.active = 1
AND c.active = 1
AND (MATCH(pl.name, pl.description_short) AGAINST("' . $ft_search_term . '" IN BOOLEAN MODE)
OR MATCH(m.name) AGAINST("' . $ft_search_term . '" IN BOOLEAN MODE))
```

[7/23/2025 1:34 PM] instazx2
what i'm wanting to build is an "event sourced" application, with Domain Driven Design.  DDD defines a core set of "Domains" that the business side and the developers use the same terminology for.  as a convention, whenever i'm talking about a domain, i'll capitalize the first letter -- and if i'm doing that, i am being VERY explicit about the terminology being used.  please do the same.


[7/23/2025 1:35 PM] instazx2
so something like "when a Customer adds a Product to their Cart, the Product should notify the Inventory that it might be sold soon"


[7/23/2025 1:38 PM] instazx2
you'll see that come up more like "when a user goes to the site should it show them their cart again" -- "user" is a word that's important here, but it's not one of the domains we're discussing.  is "user" a Customer?  a Guest?  whatever.  it doesn't need to be super performative, but the primary goal is so the code can read very similarly to how we'd talk about the business logic


[7/23/2025 1:38 PM] instazx2
AND all of my immediate questions are about the domains, so ... get comfortable with it


[7/23/2025 1:40 PM] instazx2
the way an event-sourced application works in the context of DDD, is we "tell" a domain that something happened, or that something needs to happen.  the domain will do that, and then announce that it did the thing.  other domains, if they care, can react to that.  if they react to it, they can then announce they did a thing, and so on.


[7/23/2025 1:46 PM] instazx2
it's not as convoluted as it sounds.  it's more like ...

```
interface ProductSold: {
  product_id,
  user_id,
  quantity,
  unit_price
}
```

when someone pays for their cart, gears turn, and 7 of those messages squirt out, one for each product they had in the cart.

the Inventory domain would probably be interested in knowing something was sold so it can update stock ... so it will "listen" (subscribe) to ProductSold events and use that to update inventory:
```cs
When(ProductSold)
  .Then(x => x.Data.Quantity -= x.Event.Quantity)
  .Publish(InventoryUpdated)
```


[7/23/2025 1:49 PM] brett4091
Right, this makes perfect sense. I was a hobbyist game developer and a lot of that is event based, and albeit not exactly like this, the idea was the same


[7/23/2025 1:51 PM] brett4091
Sorry to interrupt, just wanted to confirm I was following and understanding thus far!


[7/23/2025 1:53 PM] instazx2
but where this becomes immediately and immensely powerful is something like ...

```
domain PopularityContest {

  When(ProductSold)
    .Find(x => x.Event.ProductId)
    .Then(x => x.Product.Sales += 1)
    .Publish(PopularityUpdated);
}

domain ProductSearch {

  When(PopularityUpdated)
    .Find(x => x.Event.ProductId)
    .Then(x => x.Weights *= 1.02)
}
```


[7/23/2025 1:55 PM] instazx2
yep, video games are extremely event based, and they are the same idea.  you're right in that they obviously behave differently, but if you understand one you'll understand the other.


[7/23/2025 1:57 PM] instazx2
the idea with a domain-driven eventing system is, hopefully, shown with the second chunk of code there.  that's not exactly the syntax, although it's close -- but more importantly, i was able to add the idea of a product search, weighted by sale popularity, *without modifying anything in the core "take money remove inventory" code*


[7/23/2025 2:00 PM] instazx2
i know prestashop has an idea of event hooks, but i hate the way they implemented them.  most notably, they have to run all the hooks before the request finishes.  which means even if something is hooked to, like, update the search index ... the user is waiting for that to finish while they're staring at a spinner.  the search update could absolutely happen in the background, it wouldn't impact anything at all, but because of how PS is architected it can't do it easily


[7/23/2025 2:01 PM] instazx2
what MassTransit does differently is use a message broker, which is a pub/sub system with queues, and the queues support message inheritance


[7/23/2025 2:03 PM] instazx2
the queues with the message broker provide a contractual guarantee for the application -- if the broker says "i have received your message", then the broker is guaranteeing it **will** deliver that message.  which means we are very safe to chuck messages at the broker (which is very fast) and return to the user ASAP, so the end-user experience remains extremely responsive.  the code in the background is pulling the messages off the queues and processing as fast as it can, but it's completely decoupled from the user's wait


[7/23/2025 2:04 PM] instazx2
like even a kind of "ProductSold" notification that updates inventory.  we don't have to wait for the inventory count to be updated before moving on.  the inventory *will* be updated, and if there's something big and heavy happening to the inventory at that time, then it *will* be updated as soon as the big heavy thing is done


[7/23/2025 2:11 PM] instazx2
another helpful thing is the messages being inherited from each other.  like, when a product is sold, it seems reasonable to announce that a product was sold.

so we publish a `ProductSoldNotification` onto the message bus, and the notification could contain things like:  

* customer details
* product details
* inventory details
* payment details

the code that publishes doesn't know or care who's potentially listening for it.  the Customer domain might listen for that event in order to update some customer details.  the Product domain might listen to update popularity (and then publish its own update so the Search can update).  the Inventory domain might listen to update stock numbers and determine if "low product" notices need to be sent.  the Financial domain might be listening to add some accounting/audit records.  the Caching domain might be listening as well in order to update caches, etc.

[7/23/2025 2:17 PM] instazx2
ok, because messages are frequently inherited, and it makes sense to do so based on what various consumers are doing.


[7/23/2025 2:19 PM] instazx2
somewhere on the backend, there'll be code that handles the pay-for-a-cart part.  i don't know exactly where, but i would expect to see something like this:

```cs
public void PurchaseCart(UserCart cart)
{
  var chargeDetails = paymentProcessor.ChargePayment(cart.PaymentDetails);
  if (chargeDetails.Successful)
  {
    foreach(var product in cart.Products) 
    {
      endpoint.Publish<ProductSoldNotification>(product);
    }
  }
}
```


[7/23/2025 2:20 PM] instazx2
yeah, a whole lot is handwaved away, but that's about where/how the messages get published.  there'll be a bit more there, but that's overall what it looks like


[7/23/2025 2:27 PM] instazx2
now, something like the Customer domain might care about this, just so it can update a "total spend" amount for fun.  does this mean that every time we add some kind of message, all the various domains need to be updated to specifically handle that message?  yes, but not really?

on the Customer domain, the "total spend" really just cares about PaymentReceived vs PaymentRefunded.  and a whole lot of different things could cause PaymentReceived or PaymentRefunded, and that's where the message inheritance comes from:

```cs
interface PaymentReceived 
{
  Guid CustomerId;
  decimal PaymentAmount;
}

interface PaymentRefunded
{
  Guid CustomerId;
  decimal RefundAmount;
}

interface StockChanged
{
  Guid InventoryId;
  int Quantity;
}

interface ProductSold
{
  Guid ProductId;
  decimal Price;
}

class ProductSoldNotification : PaymentReceived, StockChanged, ProductSold
{
  Guid CustomerId;
  Guid InventoryId;
  int Quantity;
  decimal Price;
  decimal PaymentAmount = Quantity * Price;
}
```

this is sorta messy and jammed together, but what i'm trying to show is that there might be several different types of events, and they all roll up into a single message that gets published.  each domain just listens for their *part* of the message and reacts accordingly

```cs
class Inventory
{

}

class Product
{

  string Name { get; set; }
  string Description { get; set; }

  Inventory? Inventory { get; set; }

}
```

[7/23/2025 3:01 PM] instazx2
ok, well, lemme show the related code so far for Inventory.  it's broken into a few sections:

* Commands / Events
* Database Model
* State Machine
* API Endpoints

the API endpoints are what your code would call, directly.  they're HTTPS endpoints and you'd interact with them with `$http.get()` / `$http.post()`.  the endpoints take the user payloads, validate and transform them into messages, and push the messages onto the bus.

the messages are handled by the State Machine, which contextually updates the Database Model.  afterwards, the State Machine might send a message back to a waiting endpoint (to return to the UI), or it might announce changes to the system as a whole, or both.


[7/23/2025 3:01 PM] instazx2
do you have a preference on which end to start with?  i'm leaning towards starting with the API side, and walking down and back up


[7/23/2025 3:01 PM] brett4091
I'm good with starting at endpoints


[7/23/2025 3:03 PM] instazx2
okie dokie


[7/23/2025 3:04 PM] instazx2
so the job of the endpoints is to interface directly with "the public".  they handle the user authentication, authorization, and validating the request payloads.  what's really nice about that kind of separation is that the bus is completely behind the firewall -- so we can publish whatever super sensitive data we want to onto the bus, and it only pachinko-machines around the backend


[7/23/2025 3:04 PM] instazx2
@Kbreezy - KB3D  were we supposed to be on a google chat?  because this with Brett rn is basically what i wanted that chat for


[7/23/2025 3:06 PM] instazx2
the one endpoint i have so far is intended to be more from the admin portal, which is "create inventory".  my intent for this endpoint is more like "i am transitioning from $oldsystem to $newsystem, and i need to enter part numbers and quantity"


[7/23/2025 3:09 PM] instazx2
so i start with mapping some endpoints:

```cs
public static class InventoryEndpoints
{
    public static void MapTo(WebApplication app)
    {
        app.MapPost("/inventory", Create);
        app.MapGet("/inventory/{partNumber}", GetById);
        app.MapPatch("/inventory/{partNumber}/quantity/{quantity:int}", UpdateQuantity);
        app.MapDelete("/inventory/{partNumber}", Delete);
    }
}
```

from here you should be able to see some of the RESTful collection-style URLs.  so lets look at Create


[7/23/2025 3:10 PM] instazx2
Create endpoint:
```cs
public static class InventoryEndpoints
{
    public static async Task<IResult> Create(
        [FromBody] CreateInventoryPayload payload,
        [FromServices] IRequestClient<CreateInventoryRequest> client,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)
    {
        if (!payload.IsValid())
            return Results.BadRequest();

        Response response = await client.GetResponse<CreateInventoryResponse, CreateInventoryFailure>(new
        {
            payload.PartNumber,
            payload.Description,
            payload.StockQuantity,
            payload.InventoryStatus
        }, cancellationToken);

        return response switch
        {
            (_, CreateInventoryResponse success) => Results.Ok(success),
            (_, CreateInventoryFailure failure) => Results.Conflict(failure),
            _ => Results.InternalServerError("Unexpected response type from backend")
        };
    }
}
```

Payload object:
```cs
public class CreateInventoryPayload
{
    public string? PartNumber { get; set; }
    public string? Description { get; set; }
    public int StockQuantity { get; set; }
    public InventoryStatus InventoryStatus { get; set; }

    public bool IsValid() 
        => !string.IsNullOrEmpty(PartNumber) && StockQuantity >= 0;
}
```

yep.  now inside the actual endpoint code, i'll go piece-by-piece.  here it is again:

```cs
    public static async Task<IResult> Create(
        [FromBody] CreateInventoryPayload payload,
        [FromServices] IRequestClient<CreateInventoryRequest> client,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken = default)
    {
        if (!payload.IsValid())
            return Results.BadRequest();

        Response response = await client.GetResponse<CreateInventoryResponse, CreateInventoryFailure>(new
        {
            payload.PartNumber,
            payload.Description,
            payload.StockQuantity,
            payload.InventoryStatus
        }, cancellationToken);

        return response switch
        {
            (_, CreateInventoryResponse success) => Results.Ok(success),
            (_, CreateInventoryFailure failure) => Results.Conflict(failure),
            _ => Results.InternalServerError("Unexpected response type from backend")
        };
    }
```

```cs
public interface CreateInventoryRequest : BaseInventoryCommand
{
```

the `BaseInventoryCommand` superclass is what i use for all Inventory-related commands, and looks like:
```cs
public interface BaseInventoryCommand

}
```

```
CreateInventoryRequest
```

an interface is an object that, by itself, does nothing -- but applied to other objects it tells the rest of the code "if you ask this thingy to do these things, it's guaranteeing it can".  it can be used for both functions and fields, but in the case of messaging they're *only* fields.  messages aren't executable, they just contain structured data

[7/23/2025 4:11 PM] instazx2
a very common way to write inventory-management code could be something like this ... and i'm guessing this is just 100% natural looking for you:

```php
function createInventory($payload) {

  let result = mysqli_query('SELECT * FROM inventory WHERE partNumber = ? LIMIT 1', new { $payload.PartNumber });
  if (result != null)
    throw new DuplicateItemException();

  mysqli_query('INSERT INTO inventory VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?)', $payload);
}
```


[7/23/2025 4:12 PM] instazx2
so we first check to see if there's an existing part number, and error if it is  (remember this is "create new item", so an existing item with that pn is a problem).  then we insert a single row with the values we got sent


[7/23/2025 4:12 PM] instazx2
i'm using this as a starting point, so i can slowly nudge it around into a domain, but small enough pieces at once that you can watch the whole process


[7/23/2025 4:13 PM] brett4091
Yup, that looks exactly like what I would expect for a createInventory func


[7/23/2025 4:16 PM] instazx2
what about "updateStockQuantity"?  my take on it is something like:

```php
function updateStockQuantity($inventoryId, $quantity) {

  let $inventory = mysqli_query('SELECT * FROM inventory WHERE id = ? LIMIT 1', new { $inventoryId });
  if ($inventory == null)
    throw new MissingItemException();

  // can't set quantity for on-demand items, so this is an error
  if ($inventory.Status == OnDemand)
    throw new InventoryException();

  $inventory.StockQuantity = $quantity;

  mysqli_query('UPDATE inventory SET partNumber = ?, description = ?, stockQuantity = ?, Status = ? WHERE id = ?', $inventory);
}
```


[7/23/2025 4:17 PM] instazx2
we have to load the existing inventory item to make sure it exists, and setting the quantity is also something that's allowed.  if so, we update the quantity, and immediately save the item back to the DB


[7/23/2025 4:20 PM] instazx2
i realize the more common way this would likely be done is with a much more complex WHERE clause on the query, directly in the UPDATE.  like:

```php
function updateStockQuantity($inventoryId, $quantity) {

  let rows = mysqli_query('UPDATE inventory SET stockQuantity = ? WHERE id = ? AND status = ?', new { $quantity, $inventoryId, 'InStock' });

  return $rows == 1;
}
```

but there's reasons to not do it that way, and opt for doing more of the logic in code


[7/23/2025 4:22 PM] instazx2
and that's more a matter of scaling.  "update stock quantity" is a task that involves modifying 0 or 1 rows in the database.  no matter what, MySql has to find the row.  by structuring the code to simplify the WHERE clause, MySql can find the row and stop.  it doesn't have to do anything else, it can find the data and give it to the application.  the application can then do whatever complicated bullshit it needs to in order to decide if it's ok to change the `quantity` value, and once that decision is made then it updates just that one row.

the "do it in code" logic means that MySql only has to do primary-key lookups, and no scans or using other indexes


[7/23/2025 4:24 PM] instazx2
in the "do it all in the where clause" version, we are asking MySql to both find the item, and then make decisions about it.  by moving the logic into code, instead of MySql trying to decide if the WHERE clause matches all the various status checks, MySql can move on to the next "find by ID"


[7/23/2025 4:24 PM] instazx2
so it allows more concurrent users, since the software side can participate more, and the database side is just loading and saving single rows at a time


[7/23/2025 4:24 PM] instazx2
make sense?


[7/23/2025 4:25 PM] brett4091

```php
function updateStockQuantity($inventoryId, $quantity) {

  let $inventory = mysqli_query('SELECT * FROM inventory WHERE id = ? LIMIT 1', new { $inventoryId });
  if ($inventory == null)
    throw new MissingItemException();

function updateDescription($inventoryId, $description) {

  let $inventory = mysqli_query('SELECT * FROM inventory WHERE id = ? LIMIT 1', new { $inventoryId });
  if ($inventory == null)
    throw new MissingItemException();

  // description can always be updated
  $inventory.Description = $description;

  mysqli_query('UPDATE inventory SET partNumber = ?, description = ?, stockQuantity = ?, Status = ? WHERE id = ?', $inventory);
```

[7/23/2025 4:28 PM] instazx2
but i'm also seeing a lot of repetition.  what if we made some methods and pulled out the duplicated code?

```php
function updateStockQuantity($inventoryId, $quantity) {
  let $inventory = load($inventoryId);

  // can't set quantity for on-demand items, so this is an error
  if ($inventory.Status == OnDemand)
    throw new InventoryException();

  $inventory.StockQuantity = $quantity;
  save($inventory);
}

function updateDescription($inventoryId, $description) {
  let $inventory = load($inventoryId);

  // description can always be updated
  $inventory.Description = $description;
  save($inventory);
}

function load($inventoryId) {
  let $inventory = mysqli_query('SELECT * FROM inventory WHERE id = ? LIMIT 1', new { $inventoryId });
  if ($inventory == null)
    throw new MissingItemException();

  return $inventory;
}

function save($inventory) {
  mysqli_query('UPDATE inventory SET partNumber = ?, description = ?, stockQuantity = ?, Status = ? WHERE id = ?', $inventory);
}
```
