[7/23/2025 4:44 PM] instazx2
the "saga" is the class that's 1:1 with the table in the database.  this is the one for Inventory:

```cs
public sealed class InventorySaga : SagaStateMachineInstance
{
    public Guid CorrelationId { get; set; }
    public uint RowVersion { get; set; }
    public int CurrentState { get; set; }

    public string PartNumber { get; set; } = "";
    public string Description { get; set; } = "";
    public int StockQuantity { get; set; }
    public InventoryStatus InventoryStatus { get; set; }
}
```

it's got a few extra things in it that we haven't mentioned before, like RowVersion and CurrentState.  those are there because MassTransit needs to do some housekeeping behind the scenes, and it needs those properties.  the ones we care about are clearly right there though


[7/23/2025 4:45 PM] instazx2
the matching table in the DB would be 7 columns wide


[7/23/2025 4:45 PM] instazx2
i don't need you to care about those housekeeping fields yet, i just want you to notice the relationships between things so far


[7/23/2025 4:46 PM] instazx2
CurrentState and InventoryStatus might actually be the same thing


[7/23/2025 4:46 PM] instazx2
anyway


[7/23/2025 4:49 PM] instazx2
this is the simplest state machine for the Inventory domain.  it doesn't actually *do* anything, but that's coming in like 3 more messages. 

```cs
public sealed class InventoryStateMachine : MassTransitStateMachine<InventorySaga>
{
    public InventoryStateMachine()
    {
        InstanceState(m => m.CurrentState,
            Available,
            OnHold,
            Backordered,
            Discontinued
        );
    }
    
    public State Available { get; }
    public State OnHold { get; }
    public State Backordered { get; }
    public State Discontinued { get; }    
}
```

the "InstanceState" thing is telling MassTransit "hey you can keep track of the state of this saga by using the CurrentState property, and these are the states i want you to care about"


[7/23/2025 4:49 PM] instazx2
again, this doesn't actually *do* anything yet, it's just ... there.  are there any bits *so far* that are confusing?  or should i move on and start adding "things"

[7/23/2025 4:54 PM] instazx2
and it's relevant, because this is what we add to the StateMachine:

```cs
public sealed class InventoryStateMachine : MassTransitStateMachine<InventorySaga>
{
    public InventoryStateMachine()
    {
        InstanceState(m => m.CurrentState,
            Available,
            OnHold,
            Backordered,
            Discontinued
        );
    }
    
    // notice the name ---vv
    public Event<CreateInventoryRequest> Created { get; }
    
    public State Available { get; }
    public State OnHold { get; }
    public State Backordered { get; }
    public State Discontinued { get; }    
}
```


[7/23/2025 4:55 PM] instazx2
adding that 1 line tells MassTransit:

[7/23/2025 5:05 PM] instazx2
now, the Create one was a bit weird, because we *don't* want an existing instance, and we know if it's already existing by checking for an existing partNumber right?


[7/23/2025 5:05 PM] instazx2
magic that you definitely don't need to care about right now, and may not need to care about ever.  but "magic" will get us far enough


[7/23/2025 5:07 PM] instazx2
we need to tell the statemachine "for the Created event, i want you to find anything where the PartNumber matches what's in the message".  usually it's done by CorrelationId and we don't need this line at all, but for this specific event we do, because of how it works.

`Event(() => Created, e => e.CorrelateBy((s, c) => s.PartNumber == c.Message.PartNumber));`


[7/23/2025 5:09 PM] instazx2
* a CreateInventoryRequest message was published.
* the StateMachine received a copy of it
* the Event() line says "find a match by PartNumber"
* we don't have a match, so the statemachine will create a new instance for us, and run the "Initial" behaviors:

```cs
Initially(
  When(Created)
    .Then(SetProperties)
    .TransitionTo(Available)
    .RespondAsync(Message<CreateInventoryResponse>)
    .PublishAsync(Message<InventoryCreatedEvent>)
);
```


[7/23/2025 5:09 PM] instazx2
`When(Created)` is the same `Created` as the new line at the bottom


[7/23/2025 5:10 PM] instazx2
SetProperties is a little helper function to copy all the values from the message to the database model:

```cs
static void SetProperties(BehaviorContext<InventorySaga, CreateInventoryRequest> context)
{
    context.Saga.PartNumber = context.Message.PartNumber;
    context.Saga.Description = context.Message.Description;
    context.Saga.StockQuantity = context.Message.StockQuantity;
    context.Saga.InventoryStatus = context.Message.InventoryStatus;
}
```

and this is why i went through the PHP example above, by slowly morphing "we have the ID and load the object ourselves" to "somebody else loads the object for us and gives it to us"


[7/23/2025 5:11 PM] brett4091
Makes sense, the event communicates everything necessary and the magic somebody operating behind the *curtain takes care of the rest


[7/23/2025 5:11 PM] instazx2
putting the whole thing together, this is it:

```cs
public sealed class InventoryStateMachine : MassTransitStateMachine<InventorySaga>
{
    public InventoryStateMachine()
    {
        InstanceState(m => m.CurrentState,
            Available,
            OnHold,
            Backordered,
            Discontinued
        );

        Event(() => Created, e => e.CorrelateBy((s, c) => s.PartNumber == c.Message.PartNumber));

        Initially(
            When(Created)
                .Then(SetProperties)
                .TransitionTo(Available)
                .RespondAsync(Message<CreateInventoryResponse>)
                .PublishAsync(Message<InventoryCreatedEvent>)
        );
    }

    public Event<CreateInventoryRequest> Created { get; }

    public State Available { get; }
    public State OnHold { get; }
    public State Backordered { get; }
    public State Discontinued { get; }

    static void SetProperties(BehaviorContext<InventorySaga, CreateInventoryRequest> context)
    {
        context.Saga.PartNumber = context.Message.PartNumber;
        context.Saga.Description = context.Message.Description;
        context.Saga.StockQuantity = context.Message.StockQuantity;
        context.Saga.InventoryStatus = context.Message.InventoryStatus;
    }

    static Task<SendTuple<TMessage>> Message<TMessage>(BehaviorContext<InventorySaga> context)
        where TMessage : class, BaseInventoryEvent 
        => context.Init<TMessage>(context.Saga);
}
```

[7/23/2025 5:14 PM] instazx2
our Inventory domain now listens for CreateInventoryRequests, checks for an existing inventory, creates a new Inventory item if one wasn't found, sets all the values from the AJAX call, responds to the endpoint with "CreateInventoryResponse" so it can return a 200 OK, broadcasts to anything that cares "a new inventory item was created" (which would likely be used by the Product domain to create a single-item product)


[7/23/2025 5:14 PM] instazx2
and it does all that with full distributed asynchronous processing, durable message delivery, automatic failure retries, structured logging, metrics and tracing, and a bunch of dashboards already existing


[7/23/2025 5:15 PM] instazx2
but, really, i can run this and hit the POST endpoint with Postman or Curl, and new shit shows up in the database from just the code i've pasted to you


[7/23/2025 5:16 PM] instazx2
and since all the database code is handled by the library, it's database-agnostic.  we can run on MySql, Postgres, SQL Server, MongoDB, or any combination of them as appropriate

[7/24/2025 10:32 AM] brett4091
At the most basic, ProductBundles are just virtual containers comprised of Products.

Example:

- ProductBundle X contains 2x Product A and 4x Product B.
- Product A currently has 10 stock, Product B currently has 12 stock.
- ProductBundle X can be bought 3 times

What *could* be a little tricky with this implementation is that ProductBundles "inventory" should be recalculated based on the individual Products inventory, triggered by events such as Orders.


[7/24/2025 10:33 AM] instazx2
i am like 98% blindly confident that purchases will correctly pachinko-machine their way around to keep things consistent, and if that falls apart that's a bug not a flaw


[7/24/2025 10:34 AM] instazx2
thoughts on things like:

* Inventory represents the physical products
* Products control how Inventory is priced and sold
* ProductBundles are the only things that can be added to carts
* A new Product creates an implicit ProductBundle with the 1 item at the original price

[7/24/2025 10:50 AM] brett4091
I may be misunderstanding your intention for ProductBundles, but for now I am assuming these are pack equivalents. With that said, these are my thoughts, but you and Kris may disagree

- Inventory always represents physical products. Digital products like manuals, gift cards, etc. and Services have no such restriction. If they do, the allocations should be tracked separately from Inventory.

- Pricing is at the product level. Inventory is strictly the quantity of the physical item available.

- Products and ProductBundles can both be added to carts. ProductBundles are a collection of Products, grouped for simplicity and potentially "bought together" discounts.
Hardware packs like the [Sherpa Heavy Extruder - Hardware Pack - RIGDA GearSet](https://kb-3d.com/store/extruders-hotends/1389-sherpa-heavy-extruder-hardware-pack-rigda-gearset-1723833309605.html) provide a good example. It contains a bunch of individual Products, customers could add all of these to the cart separately, but the ProductBundles save them the trouble.

- A new Product does not inherently create a ProductBundle. The ProductBundle is an optional way to group Products, but 99% of the time Products will be standalone

[7/24/2025 2:56 PM] instazx2
oh, another thing that's worth mentioning ...

consider that pretty much everything with MassTransit happens because a message got pushed onto the bus.  everything gets routed properly and what-not, but things happen because a message got pushed to a bus.

sooooo ... there's a really natural thing, where messages can be scheduled or delayed.  since prestashop is a web app, there's no "background", and it has to just steal occasional visits to run background / cleanup tasks.  but MassTransit can just schedule a message to be sent at some point in the future and it happens completely on its own


[7/24/2025 3:05 PM] instazx2
```cs
public class CartStateMachine : MassTransitStateMachine<CartSaga>
{
  public CartStateMachine()
  {
    Schedule(() => CartAbandoned, t => t.Delay = TimeSpan.FromDays(14));

    Initially(
      When(ItemAdded)
        .Then(c => c.Saga.Items.Add(c.Message.Item))
        .Schedule(CartAbandoned, c => c.Init<CartActivityTimeout>(c.CorrelationId))
        .TransitionTo(Active)
        .Publish<CartModified>()
    );

    During(Active,
      When(CartAbandoned).Finalize(), // deletes from the database
      When(ItemAdded)
        .Then(c => c.Saga.Items.Add(c.Message.Item))
        .Unschedule(CartAbandoned)    // cancel the old timer and restart it
        .Schedule(CartAbandoned, c => c.Init<CartActivityTimeout>(c.CorrelationId)),
      When(ItemRemoved)
        .Then(c => c.Saga.Items.Remove(c.Message.Item))
        .Unschedule(CartAbandoned)    // cancel the old timer and restart it
        .Schedule(CartAbandoned, c => c.Init<CartActivityTimeout>(c.CorrelationId)),
    );
  }

  public State Active { get; }

  public Schedule<CartSaga, CartActivityTimeout> CartAbandoned { get; }
  public Event<CartItemAdded> ItemAdded { get; }
  public Event<CartItemRemoved> ItemRemoved { get; }
}
```


[7/24/2025 3:06 PM] instazx2
so in that code, interacting with the cart via the ItemAdded / ItemRemoved events will make it set a 14-day timer to have the cart delete itself


[7/24/2025 3:06 PM] instazx2
and that's just managed by the message broker, nothing that my/our code has to really care about

[7/24/2025 3:50 PM] instazx2
@Brett - KB3D   😘 

install:
```sql
CREATE TABLE ps_search_aggregate
(
    id INT AUTO_INCREMENT NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY,
    id_product INT NOT NULL,
    id_lang INT NOT NULL,
    id_shop INT NOT NULL,
    id_image INT NULL,
    product_name VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    product_description VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    category_name VARCHAR(255) NULL,
    manufacturer_name VARCHAR(255) NULL,
    price DECIMAL(8,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.0,
    quantity INT NOT NULL,
    link_rewrite VARCHAR(255) NULL,
    weight DECIMAL(8,2) NOT NULL DEFAULT 0.0,
    combined TEXT NULL,
    FULLTEXT combined_idx (combined)    
) ENGINE=InnoDB;
```

run on "change" event:
```sql
TRUNCATE TABLE ps_search_aggregate;
INSERT INTO ps_search_aggregate (
    id_product,
    id_lang,
    id_shop,
    id_image,
    product_name,
    product_description,
    category_name,
    manufacturer_name,
    price,
    quantity,
    link_rewrite,
    weight,
    combined
) SELECT
    p.id_product,
    pl.id_lang,
    ps.id_shop,
    i.id_image,
    pl.name AS product_name,
    IF (
        LENGTH(pl.description_short) > 253,
        CONCAT(LEFT(pl.description_short, 250), '...'),
        pl.description_short
    ) AS product_description,
    cl.name AS category_name,
    m.name AS manufacturer_name,
    p.price,
    sa.quantity,    
    pl.link_rewrite,
    COALESCE(w.calculated_weight, 0) + (COALESCE(w.manual_boost, 0) * 100) AS weight,
    CONCAT(
        COALESCE(pl.name, ''), ' ', 
        COALESCE(pl.description_short, ''), ' ', 
        COALESCE(cl.name, ''), ' ', 
        COALESCE(m.name, '')
    ) AS combined
FROM `ps_product` p
LEFT JOIN `ps_product_lang` pl ON (p.id_product = pl.id_product)
LEFT JOIN `ps_category_lang` cl ON (p.id_category_default = cl.id_category)
LEFT JOIN `ps_manufacturer` m ON (p.id_manufacturer = m.id_manufacturer)
LEFT JOIN `ps_stock_available` sa ON (p.id_product = sa.id_product AND sa.id_product_attribute = 0)
LEFT JOIN `ps_image` i ON (p.id_product = i.id_product AND i.cover = 1)
LEFT JOIN `ps_product_shop` ps ON (p.id_product = ps.id_product)
LEFT JOIN `ps_category` c ON (p.id_category_default = c.id_category)
LEFT JOIN `ps_prestosearch_weights` w ON (p.id_product = w.product_id)
WHERE p.active = 1 AND ps.active = 1 AND c.active = 1;
```

"new" search:
```sql
SELECT *, MATCH(combined) AGAINST ('Bondtech' IN BOOLEAN MODE) AS relevance_score
FROM ps_search_aggregate
WHERE 
  MATCH(combined) AGAINST ('Bondtech' IN BOOLEAN MODE)
ORDER BY 
  (weight + relevance_score) DESC,
  price DESC,
  product_name ASC
  LIMIT 25;
```

although the search part is missing the additional parts you had for "exact match = 10000 relevance" and the mroe rigid filters like price.  it seems like the ordering should be weight * relevance, not weight + relevance, but it's easy to play with

[7/25/2025 10:06 AM] instazx2
the fulltext search (for both innodb and elasticsearch) use Natural Language Processing for tokenization and memoization of the text


[7/25/2025 10:06 AM] instazx2
it already knows about "raspberry", but it has no idea what "voron" or "nevermore" are


[7/25/2025 10:06 AM] instazx2
so it considers those as very important words, and will naturally boost the relevance of them


[7/25/2025 10:07 AM] brett4091
ahh interesting!


[7/25/2025 10:07 AM] instazx2
we are in a domain where "raspberry" is actually a relevant thing and not just a foodstuff, so we could (maybe) actually just tell the underlying NLP engine about "raspberry"


[7/25/2025 10:08 AM] instazx2
the boosts are a very viable solution and you will also want them


[7/25/2025 10:08 AM] instazx2
but there will be a few words that are specific to our market/domain that the NLP engine isn't expecting, and providing the boosts/debuffs to those words in general will help shape the results


[7/25/2025 10:09 AM] brett4091
For sure, and having the basics analytics in place, I can check frequent queries just to make sure the ordering is how we want it

[7/25/2025 10:17 AM] instazx2
ok so i'm using a thing called ".NET Aspire".  it's whole job is to help build distributed applications using Docker, so it combines container management with centralized structured logging, trace captures (*), and overall performance metrics


[7/25/2025 10:19 AM] instazx2
\*: the trace captures are cool as hell.  you can follow a single random web request through every single step.  like, browser to server.  server to message bus.  you can see how long it sat in the queues, until it got picked up for processing.  how long did the processing take?  how long did we spend waiting on the database to reply?  *what logs were written specific to this request?*


[7/25/2025 10:20 AM] instazx2
but, i mention all that, because the whole application is already a bunch of Docker containers connected together ... so, does it make sense that feature-extending plugins would be distributed as their own little "side-car" Docker containers, and they attach themselves like lampreys to the message queues?


[7/25/2025 10:21 AM] instazx2
like the 'full text search'.  i don't really intend to build that in as core functionality.  i intend to create a new event handler and listen for every single "ProductUpdated" message that comes out and update my search index from that.  but this can 100% be entirely self-contained ... with its own tiny little application, its own tiny little fulltext-optimized database, maybe even its own little Redis instance


[7/25/2025 10:23 AM] instazx2
this is the dashboard Aspire gives us.  i've grouped the pieces how i see them grouped (and i'll see if there's even better grouping available).  but i'm envisioning something like the large red braces to be what a "plugin" looks like -- a handful of little things all grouped together, and added or removed by wholesale attaching or removing a new docker image

[7/25/2025 10:59 AM] instazx2
MassTransit *requires* each object to have a `CorrelationId UUID` property, and Inventory / Products are no exception.  if we're ok with a single, unique `PartNumber` for Inventory, then there's two ways i can use that:

1) use middleware to create a hashed value from the `PartNumber`, and assign that hashed value to the `CorrelationId`
2) let MassTransit create its own `CorrelationId` values, and the `PartNumber` is just checked for uniqueness when creating a new item

each has pros/cons, which i'll add now ...


[7/25/2025 11:07 AM] instazx2
for #1, the advantage is that we can just use the PartNumber *as* the primary key for all intents and purposes.  all messages would just use the PartNumber.  this also means that you'd never really see the ID in the URL for anything, although that may not actually be a problem (the API urls are not the same as the search engine urls)
```cs
await context.Publish<UpdateInventoryRequest>(new { PartNumber = "FAST_M3X20", StockQuantity = 10000 });
```

the downside is a `PartNumber` becomes an explicit requirement for every single `Inventory` item, because it must have a unique value to hash from.  `PartNumber` could not be reused or duplicated in any way, even if you discontinue one thing and create a new inventory item with the same `PartNumber` (either the new one would end up 'resurrecting' the deactivated one, or you'd create a shitload of orphan data elsewhere).  the other, smaller, disadvantage is that the hashed ID is completely random, which puts some extra pressure on the database when inserting new rows.

----

for #2, the advantage is that `PartNumber` becomes "soft-unique", which allows supporting things like old deactivated products, and new ones with the same exact part number.  we can still prevent duplicate part numbers, but we have more control over when they're actually considered duplicate.  it also allows us to generate 'sequential' unique IDs, which helps the database out when inserting new rows or indexing.

the disadvantage is you'd have to use the Guid ID for everything except "Create" (and when creating, you'd need to pay attention to the ID that gets returned).  so that ends up more like:
```cs
await context.Publish<UpdateInventoryRequest>(new { CorrelationId = "abab6620-16c4-49a5-a278-d7a589738ba2", StockQuantity = 10000 });
```


[7/25/2025 11:08 AM] instazx2
i generally prefer the second one for the additional control over when it's duplicate (and the extra bit of help for the DB), but i wanted your take on it


[7/25/2025 11:11 AM] instazx2
again the ID vs PartNumber is really only for the code itself and how the references are passed around.  we can absolutely make `kb-3d.com/products/FAST_M3X20` work, although we may want to go a step further and support fully-customized URL fragments


[7/25/2025 11:18 AM] brett4091
I think I would opt for the second route as well. I wouldn't exactly forsee the downside of option #1 being a common problem, but I can absolutely imagine edge cases where that could throw a wrench in things


[7/25/2025 11:18 AM] instazx2
well, that decision also applies to the other domains as well for consistency


[7/25/2025 11:19 AM] instazx2

[7/29/2025 8:36 AM] instazx2
i have 3 different scenarios that are each similar, and i'm curious if you'll still think that after i list 'em out


[7/29/2025 8:43 AM] instazx2
my naive words for them are module, plug-in, and provider:

**module**: something that becomes part of the core application directly.  hardest to write, fastest type of code, can directly access the "main" database (*), etc.  connects directly to the service bus and participates directly in everything.  also connects directly to the API layer and directly handles calls from the frontend.

**plug-in**: code that lives in a little "side car", and uses the API client to add functionality.  maybe something like a bot to interact with the backoffice part via Discord + ChatGPT?  easier to write, less restrictions on the language used.  relies on existing modules to provide the hooks it can use.

**provider**: code that overrides a specific piece of functionality.  login / user registration, payment processors, email senders, etc.


[7/29/2025 8:44 AM] instazx2

[7/29/2025 8:47 AM] brett4091

[7/29/2025 9:14 AM] instazx2
the "Web Layer" is C#/ASP.NET and handles things like:
* user registration
* user authentication / authorization
* user login
* user input validation
* most caching
* certificates / SSL
* any of the bullshit involving JavaScript


the "Backend Layer" is C#/MassTransit and handles things like:
* Domain management / logic
* major integrations with other systems
* report generation


[7/29/2025 9:17 AM] instazx2
so the Web Layer is responsible for determining who's making the current request, what they're allowed to do, and if they have the right information to do what they're trying to do.

once the Web has checked all those boxes off, it pushes the appropriate message into RabbitMQ for whatever's trying to happen.

this means the Backend can just assume that any message it gets from RabbitMQ is cool and valid, and it should process it immediately.

the Backend then does whatever the message is asking it to do, which likely involves a bit of chatter to the "transactional" db and to/from other modules.

once everything is done and settled down, the "transactional" db has fat queries with big joins sending copies of the data over to the "read models" DB

the Web uses the "read models" DB to handle any requests for "lists" or "searches" of things


[7/29/2025 9:18 AM] instazx2
would a code example maybe be easier to follow?


[7/29/2025 9:23 AM] instazx2
endpoint:
```cs
public static async Task<IResult> Create(
  CreatePayload payload,
  IUserContext context,
  IPublishEndpoint endpoint)
{
  // handle validation
  if (! payload.IsValid())
    return Results.BadRequest();

  // handle authentication
  if (context.User is null)
    return Results.Unauthorized();

  // handle authorization
  if (! context.User.Roles.Contains(Roles.Create))
    return Results.Forbidden();

  // send it on to the backend
  await endpoint.Publish<CreateItem>(payload);

  return Results.Ok();
}
```

backend:
```cs
public async Task Consume(ConsumeContext<CreateItem> context)
{
  var item = context.Message;

  // insert and save the new item
  await db.InsertAsync(item);
  await db.SaveChangesAsync();

  // announce to everything interested we created a new item with the details included
  await context.Publish<ItemCreated>(item);
}
```


[7/29/2025 9:24 AM] instazx2
in the code example, the web side does a fair bit of work, but all it's doing is a bunch of checks to see if it's ok to send it on.  the web layer has no fucking clue what's actually happening.  once it gets to the backend, that code can just care about what to do with the data.  it doesn't need to care who, where, when, why, or how (although that information can and would be included in the message)


[7/29/2025 9:30 AM] brett4091
I'm going to need a caffeine injection today, your mspaint drawing is perfect, that's about where my brain is in the mornings


[7/29/2025 9:31 AM] instazx2
also the split databases.  that's kind of a weird one, but i think it'll be ok.  

since all the messages to the backend go into a message queue, there's that whole "queue" word.  which means that things are processed in roughly the order they occur, and when it comes to a specific ID, they are guaranteed to process sequentially.

so even in cases of extremely high traffic on a contentious item (like, Kris runs a "first 10 orders are $0.01" promotion, because he's a dumbass), there might be hundreds of simultaneous orders going through, and maybe 40 of those people have an expensive kit in their cart ... but there's only 10 in stock.  even if all 40 people saw "10 in stock" on the site, and all 40 people clicked "Buy Now" at about the same time, all 40 orders will get processed sequentially.  so orders 1-10 will be fine, and orders 11-40 will fail a few milliseconds/seconds/hours later for stock problems

[7/29/2025 9:36 AM] instazx2
the "transactional" DB might even be a NoSQL, since there isn't really a reason for it to be a SQL database with tables in it.  it doesn't matter.  nothing joins to it.  the 'backend' DB is just a high-speed pile of numbers and simple text for other code to talk to as numbers and simple text.

there is code that basically just eavesdrops on the message bus constantly, and listens for things happening.  as items are Created, or Updated, or Deleted -- this other code uses those things to update its own actual SQL tables.  these tables are wide, pre-joined, and indexed up the wazoo.  don't get the idea that it's some "twice a day" sync, it will be nearly instant, just not exactly instant.


[7/29/2025 9:37 AM] instazx2
you've been fighting performance with PrestaShop for awhile, and you've also watched me come in and fight its performance as well.  no matter what, the DB has a competing set of requirements:

* we need the tables small so they're fast to update (especially things like quantity or prices!)
* we need the tables indexed for fast joins and lookups

but small tables need lots of joins, and lots of indexes slow down any kind of update


[7/29/2025 9:40 AM] instazx2
@Brett - KB3D  lemme know when you're appropriately caffeinated and i can continue braindumping


[7/29/2025 9:49 AM] instazx2
as a fairly real-world example of the transactional vs read:

backend (transactional) tables:
```sql
CREATE TABLE Inventory
(
  CorrelationId GUID NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY,
  PartNumber VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  StockQuantity INT NOT NULL,
  Status TINYINT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE Products
(
  CorrelationId GUID NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY,
  InventoryId GUID NOT NULL,
  SKU VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  Description TEXT,
  ProductImageUrl VARCHAR(255),
  ItemQuantity INT,
  ItemPrice DECIMAL(8,2)
);
```

message published.  notice that rather than "inventoryId", the message contains the actual inventory item itself nested in there.  the join was already handled
```js
"ProductCreated": {
  "correlationId": "3b6c4252-b952-44a4-bc1b-37b7617e4114",
  "inventory": {
    "correlationId": "8387da4b-c1a8-4b77-b4b8-56aac47b8a82",
    "partNumber": "FAST_M3X20",
    "stockQuantity": 9984,
    "status": "InStock"
  },
  "sku": "FAST_M3X20_1",
  "description": "Single bolt because your dumb-ass kept losing them",
  "productImageUrl": "/images/bolt_single.jpg",
  "itemQuantity": 1,
  "itemPrice": 0.99
}
```

the read-model consumer.  it really does't do much, it just listens for this message and saves it to the "readDb"
```cs
public class ReadProductConsumer : IConsumer<ProductCreated>
{
  public async Task Consume(ConsumeContext<ProductCreated> context)
  {
    await readDb.InsertAsync(context.Message.Flatten());
    await readDb.SaveChangesAsync();
  }
}
```

that table would likely look something like this.  wider columns, more text, more useful information that the web wants.
```sql
CREATE TABLE read_Products
(
  ProductId GUID NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY,
  PartNumber VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  StockQuantity INT NOT NULL,
  Status TINYINT NOT NULL,
  SKU VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  Description TEXT,
  ProductImageUrl VARCHAR(255),
  ItemQuantity INT,
  ItemPrice DECIMAL(8,2),
  FinalPrice DECIMAL(8,2)
);
```

and then the `GET /products` or `GET /products/search?term=...` queries beat up on the "read_Products" table, leaving the backend transactional tables alone

Actor model
The actor model in computer science is a mathematical model of concurrent computation that treats an actor as the basic building block of concurrent computation. In response to a message it receives, an actor can: make local decisions, create more actors, send more messages, and determine how to respond to the next message received. Actors may m...

[7/29/2025 2:48 PM] instazx2
the saga is the properties specific to an actor instance.

```cs
public sealed class InventorySaga : SagaStateMachineInstance
{
    public Guid CorrelationId { get; set; }
    public uint RowVersion { get; set; }
    public int CurrentState { get; set; }

    public string PartNumber { get; set; } = "";
    public string Description { get; set; } = "";
    public int StockQuantity { get; set; }
    public InventoryStatus InventoryStatus { get; set; }
}
```
these are the properties that are saved/loaded in the DB, and they're unique by `CorrelationId`.


[7/29/2025 2:49 PM] instazx2
the statemachine is the definition of what actions can be done depending on the current state of the actor:
```cs
public sealed class InventoryStateMachine : MassTransitStateMachine<InventorySaga>
{
    public InventoryStateMachine()
    {
        InstanceState(m => m.CurrentState,
            Available,
            OnHold,
            Backordered,
            Discontinued
        );

        Event(() => Created, e => e.CorrelateBy((s, c) => s.PartNumber == c.Message.PartNumber));

        Initially(
            When(Created)
                .Then(SetProperties)
                .TransitionTo(Available)
                .RespondAsync(Message<CreateInventoryResponse>)
                .PublishAsync(Message<InventoryCreated>)
        );

        During(Available,
            When(QuantityUpdated)
                .Then(c => c.Saga.StockQuantity = c.Message.StockQuantity)
                .RespondAsync(Message<UpdateInventoryResponse>)
                .PublishAsync(Message<InventoryQuantityUpdated>)
        );

        During(Available,
            When(Held)
                .TransitionTo(OnHold)
                .RespondAsync(Message<HoldInventoryResponse>)
                .PublishAsync(Message<InventoryHeld>)
        );
 . . .
```


[7/29/2025 2:50 PM] instazx2
publishing a message is how you tell an actor what to do:
```cs
await context.Publish<CreateInventory>(new {
  CorrelationId = "1a3c8ac5-c2df-4d5e-beea-c76043386ab4",
  PartNumber = "FAST_M3X20",
  StockQuantity = 10000
});
```

and then the combination of the statemachine logic + existing state determines what happens

[7/29/2025 2:56 PM] instazx2
The key difference is that MassTransit sagas are specifically designed for orchestrating business workflows and typically have a finite lifecycle, while the Actor model is a more general-purpose concurrency pattern. **However, the fundamental principles of message-driven, isolated state management are identical in both approaches.**

**Objects ↔ Consumers/Sagas**
Traditional game objects mirror MassTransit consumers and sagas in their object-oriented approach. Just as a game object might inherit from GameObject and override methods like Update() or OnCollision(), MassTransit consumers inherit from IConsumer<T> and implement Consume() methods. Both encapsulate state and behavior together - a game object has health/position data with movement/combat methods, while a saga has workflow state with transition logic. Both can suffer from inheritance complexity as systems grow.

**Entities ↔ Messages + Correlation**
The ECS pattern strongly parallels MassTransit's message-driven architecture. In ECS, entities are just IDs that components attach to, processed by systems that operate on component combinations. Similarly, in MassTransit, correlation IDs act like entity IDs, with different message types serving as "components" that carry pure data. Consumer services act like ECS systems, processing messages based on type and content. A saga correlating multiple message types (OrderCreated, PaymentProcessed, InventoryReserved) mirrors an entity with multiple components (Position, Health, Renderer) being processed by different systems.

**Nodes ↔ Consumer Hierarchy/Routing**
MassTransit's consumer topology and message routing resembles a scene graph's hierarchical structure. Just as nodes can have parent-child relationships where transformations flow down the tree, MassTransit consumers can be organized hierarchically through routing topology, with messages flowing through exchanges and queues in tree-like patterns. Message headers and context flow through the consumer chain similar to how transformation matrices propagate through node hierarchies. Consumer filters and middleware create processing pipelines analogous to how parent nodes affect child node processing.

[7/29/2025 3:09 PM] instazx2

[7/29/2025 3:16 PM] brett4091

[7/29/2025 3:17 PM] brett4091

[7/29/2025 3:17 PM] brett4091

[7/29/2025 3:19 PM] instazx2
exactly  (which is why it was front-and-center in my mind as a solution to your search problem)


[7/29/2025 3:20 PM] brett4091
Well, it was a brilliant solution there, and seems like it would be on a larger scale too. Insanely performative for sure


[7/29/2025 3:22 PM] instazx2
(for both applications) since the 'populate the read table' happens in response to something else (instead of "nightly"), we do still have some lag.  there will always be lag, because the database is not instantly fast.  it's just with the read tables, we move the lag to "after something else" instead of the current place of "during the search"


[7/29/2025 3:25 PM] instazx2
there's still the 1 second latency, it's just now that 1 second happens in the background after somebody else has checked out, and the reads are < 0.1 sec.  

with the "do it from the live tables" strategy, there is no background process, and instead there's more like 0.5 seconds latency per search.  

an individual search will be faster than the "repopulate the whole search cache table", because it's doing less overall work for 10 vs 1500 rows.  but it's not 150x less work, only about 2x, so it only takes 2-3 searches against the read table before the overall performance of two tables pulls into the lead.


[7/29/2025 3:26 PM] instazx2
and even though it's not *needed* right now, i'm still thinking about the search in detail because i'm using it as a pattern for other things.


[7/29/2025 3:31 PM] instazx2
i pulled the code out a bit and moved some of it into an "InventoryManagement" module.  that's got the sagas & statemachines, endpoints, and messages for Inventory & Product.

there might be an endpoint for something like `GET /products/search?q=bondtech?page=1&size=25`.  that endpoint would probably be pretty simple, and look something like:
```cs
public static async Task<IResult> Search(string terms, int page, int skip, IProductSearchService service)
{
  var results = await service.Search(terms, page, skip);
  return Results.Ok(results);
}
```

so the endpoint isn't really doing anything *itself* for searching, it's mostly there to pull values out of the URL and pass them to the "IProductSearchService"

[7/29/2025 3:36 PM] instazx2
since my initial version of the "product search endpoint" uses an `IProductSearchService`, the InventoryManagement module could provide a fairly dumb one:

```cs
public class DefaultProductSearchService : IProductSearchService
{
  public Task<SearchResults> Search(string terms, int page, int offset)
  {
    return _db.Query("SELECT * FROM Products WHERE name LIKE '%{terms}%' OR description LIKE '%{terms}%'");
  }
}
```

then a different module could override that implementation:
```cs
public class FulltextProductSearchService : IProductSearchService
{
  public Task<SearchResults> Search(string terms, int page, int offset)
  {
    return _db.Query("SELECT * FROM read_Products WHERE MATCH(combined) AGAINST ({terms}) IN BOOLEAN MODE");
  }
}
```

and since the new code fit into the same shape as the old one, the other code using it doesn't know or care that it got swapped out


[7/29/2025 3:37 PM] instazx2
```cs
public class AgenticAiProductSearchService : IProductSearchService
{
  public Task<SearchResults> Search(string terms, int page, int offset)
  {
    return _gippity.Send("you are a helpful inventory managment assistant.  please suggest the best {offset} items for {terms}, with the most relevant ones first");
  }
}
```

[7/30/2025 10:27 AM] instazx2
@Brett - KB3D / @Kbreezy - KB3D   i do actually have a real question about how to model kits/bundles.

the way i have it now --

**Inventory** is the physical thing on the shelf.  It's mostly a backoffice thing.

**Product** is how **Inventory** is related to pricing.  There will be more rows in the **Product** table than the **Inventory** table, since I intend for quantity breaks to be implemented as a different **Product**.  (5 pack and 25 pack of a single inventory item would be two **Product**s to me)

i still want a way to represent bundles/kits.  i have hooks when a new product is created, and i can use that hook to create a bundle of 1 product, and then you could create other bundles of multiple products to represent kits.  maybe bundles are the only ones that get a retail price?  if you were selling a 3dp kit that had 5x nema17 motors, would that be a 1x 5pk of "1 motor"?  1x 1pk of "5 motors"  5x 1pk of "1 motor"?

[7/31/2025 2:53 PM] brett4091
```
    protected function pagesNotFound()
    {
        $sp = ';';
        $ln = "\n";
        $fp = fopen(_PS_ROOT_DIR_.'/modules/'.$this->name.'/csv/pagesnotfound.csv', 'w');
        $pagesNotFound = Db::getInstance()->ExecuteS(
            'SELECT DISTINCT pnf.request_uri, pnf.id_shop, su.domain '.
            'FROM '._DB_PREFIX_.'pagenotfound as pnf '.
            'LEFT JOIN '._DB_PREFIX_.'lgseoredirect as lsr '.
            'ON pnf.request_uri = lsr.url_old '.
            'INNER JOIN '._DB_PREFIX_.'shop_url as su '.
            'ON pnf.id_shop = su.id_shop '.
            'WHERE lsr.url_old IS NULL '.
            'ORDER BY pnf.date_add DESC'
        );
        $domain_base = ((!empty($_SERVER['HTTPS']) && $_SERVER['HTTPS'] != 'off')  ? 'https://' : 'http://');
        $redirect_type = '301';
        foreach ($pagesNotFound as $pageNotFound) {
            fwrite(
                $fp,
                utf8_decode($pageNotFound['request_uri']).''.$sp.$domain_base.$pageNotFound['domain'].
                $sp.$redirect_type.$sp.$pageNotFound['id_shop'].$ln
            );
        }
        fclose($fp);
        if ($pagesNotFound != false) {
            $this->html .=
                Module::DisplayConfirmation(
                    $this->l('The list of pages not found has been correctly generated,').
                    '&nbsp;<a href=../modules/'.$this->name.'/csv/pagesnotfound.csv>&nbsp;'.
                    $this->l('click here to download the CSV file').
                    '</a>.'
                );
        } else {
            $this->html .= Module::DisplayError($this->l('There are no pages not found on your shop'));
        }
    }
```

```sql
-- Total Executions: 2019260 (9.65%)
SELECT depends_on_stock
FROM `ps_stock_available`
WHERE (id_product = 724) AND (id_product_attribute = 0) AND (id_shop = 1) AND (id_shop_group = 0) LIMIT 1


-- Total Executions: 1231300 (5.89%)
SELECT out_of_stock
FROM `ps_stock_available`
WHERE (id_product = 724) AND (id_product_attribute = 0) AND (id_shop = 1) AND (id_shop_group = 0) LIMIT 1


-- Total Executions: 1230109 (5.88%)
SELECT location
FROM `ps_stock_available`
WHERE (id_product = 724) AND (id_product_attribute = 0) AND (id_shop = 1) AND (id_shop_group = 0) LIMIT 1


-- Total Executions: 1064224 (5.09%)
SELECT * FROM `ps_product_group_prices` WHERE id_product = 724 AND id_shop = 1 AND id_group = 1 LIMIT 1


-- Total Executions: 971797 (4.65%)
SELECT
            COALESCE(SUM(first_level_quantity) + SUM(pack_quantity), 0) as deep_quantity,
            COALESCE(SUM(first_level_quantity), 0) as quantity
          FROM (SELECT cp.`quantity` as first_level_quantity, 0 as pack_quantity
          FROM `ps_cart_product` cp
            WHERE cp.`id_product_attribute` = 0
            AND cp.`id_customization` = 0
            AND cp.`id_cart` = 0 AND cp.`id_product` = 724 UNION SELECT 0 as first_level_quantity, cp.`quantity` * p.`quantity` as pack_quantity
          FROM `ps_cart_product` cp JOIN `ps_pack` p ON cp.`id_product` = p.`id_product_pack` JOIN `ps_product` pr ON p.`id_product_pack` = pr.`id_product`
            WHERE cp.`id_product_attribute` = 0
            AND cp.`id_customization` = 0
            AND cp.`id_cart` = 0 AND p.`id_product_item` = 724 AND (pr.`pack_stock_type` IN (1,2) OR (
            pr.`pack_stock_type` = 3
            AND 1 = 1
        ))) as q LIMIT 1


-- Total Executions: 791723 (3.78%)
SELECT id_stock_available
FROM `ps_stock_available`
WHERE (id_product = 1824) AND (id_product_attribute = 0) AND (id_shop = 1) AND (id_shop_group = 0) LIMIT 1


-- Total Executions: 791465 (3.78%)
SELECT *
FROM `ps_stock_available` a
WHERE (a.`id_stock_available` = 12542) LIMIT 1
```

```sql
SELECT cp.id_category, COUNT(DISTINCT p.id_product) c FROM (SELECT p.id_product, p.id_manufacturer, SUM(sa.quantity) as quantity, p.condition, p.weight, p.price, psales.quantity as sales FROM ps_product p LEFT JOIN ps_product_attribute pa ON (p.id_product = pa.id_product) LEFT JOIN ps_product_attribute_combination pac ON (pa.id_product_attribute = pac.id_product_attribute) LEFT JOIN ps_stock_available sa ON (p.id_product = sa.id_product AND IFNULL(pac.id_product_attribute, 0) = sa.id_product_attribute AND sa.id_shop = 1  AND sa.id_shop_group = 0 ) LEFT JOIN ps_product_sale psales ON (psales.id_product = p.id_product) INNER JOIN ps_category_product cp ON (p.id_product = cp.id_product) INNER JOIN ps_category c ON (cp.id_category = c.id_category AND c.active=1) LEFT JOIN ps_category_group cg ON (cg.id_category = c.id_category) INNER JOIN ps_product_shop ps ON (p.id_product = ps.id_product AND ps.id_shop = 1 AND ps.active = TRUE) WHERE p.visibility IN ('both', 'catalog') AND cg.id_group='1' AND p.id_manufacturer IN (3, 63, 13, 10, 24, 21) AND c.nleft>=4 AND c.nright<=21 AND ps.id_shop='1' GROUP BY p.id_product) p INNER JOIN ps_category_product cp ON (p.id_product = cp.id_product) INNER JOIN ps_category c ON (cp.id_category = c.id_category AND c.active=1) LEFT JOIN ps_category_group cg ON (cg.id_category = c.id_category) WHERE cg.id_group='1' AND c.level_depth<=4 AND c.nleft>4 AND c.nright<21 GROUP BY cp.id_category
```

[7/31/2025 3:41 PM] instazx2
```sql
SELECT 
    cp.id_category,
    COUNT(DISTINCT p.id_product) c 
FROM (
    SELECT 
        p.id_product, 
        p.id_manufacturer, 
        SUM(sa.quantity) as quantity, 
        p.condition, 
        p.weight, 
        p.price, 
        psales.quantity as sales 
    FROM ps_product p 
    LEFT JOIN ps_product_attribute pa ON (p.id_product = pa.id_product) 
    LEFT JOIN ps_product_attribute_combination pac ON (pa.id_product_attribute = pac.id_product_attribute) 
    LEFT JOIN ps_stock_available sa ON (p.id_product = sa.id_product AND IFNULL(pac.id_product_attribute, 0) = sa.id_product_attribute AND sa.id_shop = 1  AND sa.id_shop_group = 0 ) 
    LEFT JOIN ps_product_sale psales ON (psales.id_product = p.id_product) 
    INNER JOIN ps_category_product cp ON (p.id_product = cp.id_product) 
    INNER JOIN ps_category c ON (cp.id_category = c.id_category AND c.active=1) 
    LEFT JOIN ps_category_group cg ON (cg.id_category = c.id_category) 
    INNER JOIN ps_product_shop ps ON (p.id_product = ps.id_product AND ps.id_shop = 1 AND ps.active = TRUE) 
    WHERE p.visibility IN ('both', 'catalog') 
      AND cg.id_group='1' 
      AND p.id_manufacturer IN (3, 63, 13, 10, 24, 21) 
      AND c.nleft>=4 
      AND c.nright<=21 
      AND ps.id_shop='1' 
    GROUP BY p.id_product) p 
INNER JOIN ps_category_product cp ON (p.id_product = cp.id_product) 
INNER JOIN ps_category c ON (cp.id_category = c.id_category AND c.active=1) 
LEFT JOIN ps_category_group cg ON (cg.id_category = c.id_category) 
WHERE cg.id_group='1' 
  AND c.level_depth<=4 
  AND c.nleft>4 
  AND c.nright<21 
GROUP BY cp.id_category
```

```
) ENGINE=' . _MYSQL_ENGINE_ . ' DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8;';
```

[8/1/2025 10:23 AM] brett4091 (pinned)
I would consider this to be the minimum features we need to switch from PrestaShop...

1) Product Management
- Product categories
- Product pages with images, descriptions, variants, brand, price / cost, shipping dimensions and weight, meta description, etc.
- Product discount rules
- Stock and inventory management
- ⭐ PACKS!

2) Customer Management / UX
- User accounts with registration, login and password reset
- Account portal that shows customer info, shipping addresses, order history, digital downloads, GDPR, etc.

3) Order Management
- Order processing workflow
- Order statuses
- Shipping calculations (recommend something like [BoxPacker library](https://github.com/dvdoug/BoxPacker))
- Invoice generation

4) Administration
- Dashboard for managing products, categories, customers and orders
- Staff roles and permissions
- Data import and export (***Seamless PS Import is probably single most important thing for Kris)

5) Key Points
- Payment integration with Stripe and / or Paypal
- Shipping integration with UPS and USPS (only for fetching live rates)
- Basic SEO features

{Reactions}

[8/1/2025 10:50 AM] instazx2
this is still building on "Products" being "The most basic way to package Inventory".  

so you have 250x NEMA17 motors in Inventory, as partnumber .. idk "LDO_NEMA17_60" qty 250.

then you create the following products:
* Single motor { SKU  LDO_NEMA17_1 }, qty1, price $11.99, weight 1kg
* Trident pack { SKU LDO_NEMA17_6 }, qty6, price $54.99, weight 4kg
* V2.4 pack { SKU LDO_NEMA17_7 }, qty7, price $59.99, weight 5kg
* Reseller pack { SKU LDO_NEMA17_25 }, qty25, price $124.99, weight 20kg

the products don't have stock, their stock is automatically calculated by the underlying Inventory qty and the Product's required qty.
whenever any of those Products goes into a Cart, the Product's qty is temporarily subtracted from the Inventory's qty.
whenever any of those Carts is sold, the Product qty is permanently subtracted from the Inventory qty.

[8/4/2025 12:13 PM] instazx2
for the concept of "Inventory", i've got these behaviors:

* Create ( PartNumber, Description, InitialQuantity )  -> returns ID
* IncreaseQuantity( ID, Quantity )
* DecreaseQuantity( ID, Quantity )
* ChangeDescription( ID, Description )
* Hold( ID )
* Release( ID )
* Delete( ID )
* Fetch( ID )


[8/4/2025 12:13 PM] instazx2
and i also have the various states:
* Active
* Held
* Backordered
* Discontinued

plus the 5th state of "deleted", but when it's deleted it's actually deleted so ... there isn't really a 5th state?


[8/4/2025 12:14 PM] instazx2
i'm wanting to double-check that my "if this then that" is correct


[8/4/2025 12:15 PM] instazx2
if you Create an new Inventory with a unique PartNumber, any Description, and a non-zero InitialQuantity, you'll now have an Inventory item with those values, in the "Active" status.  when it's Active, it's available to sell.


[8/4/2025 12:20 PM] brett4091
Makes sense to me. I never considered inventory having* a description, usually that's reserved for the product, but I suppose it does make sense


[8/4/2025 12:21 PM] brett4091
Held is more commonly referred to as Reserved, but that's about the only thing I can pick out


[8/4/2025 12:21 PM] instazx2
the Description is kind of a placeholder for "Inventory Metadata".  it's not intended to be the public-facing text


[8/4/2025 12:24 PM] instazx2
once you have a valid item:

* Hold() will effectively "freeze" it.  a Held Inventory item can't be interacted with at all, except getting the current properties.  this isn't "reserved in cart", it's more like an actual lock/release from modification.
* IncreaseQuantity() will add that much to the current stock and broadcast an "Updated" event
* DecreaseQuantity() has a few behaviors:
* * Trying to Decrease more than the available quantity will fail, report the failure to the caller, and leave the quantity unmodified
* * Trying to Decrease less than the available quantity works as expected, and publishes a QuantityUpdated event (which is also an InventoryUpdated event)
* Delete will immediately make it "Discontinued", and it can't be added to a cart anymore.  It's still around for reporting purposes, and eating up a part number you can't reuse.


[8/4/2025 12:26 PM] instazx2
the Backordered state is there, but it's not really used.  do we want Backordering to be a manual thing?  do we want it to happen as a result of the "StockQuantity" getting to a specific value?  if an Inventory item becomes Backordered, what does that do to all the Products that use it?


[8/4/2025 12:27 PM] instazx2
if you have a Discontinued item:
* what should happen if it still has quantity "in stock"?
* should we be able to "Recontinue" it?  if so, via what operation?  (Create?)
* calling Delete on a Discontinued item is a "no, really, delete it" and it will go away.  damn the torpedoes, damn your reporting

[8/4/2025 1:41 PM] instazx2
@Kbreezy - KB3D  i have a proposed behavior for Inventory / Products / Backordering that i think makes more sense, and wanted to confirm this would work for your scenarios.

**Inventory**
* Represents physical thingy on a shelf
* Not sold by itself
* Primarily used for quantity tracking
* Can automatically Backorder with triggers
* *Quantity is never negative*

**PhysicalProduct**
* Makes Inventory sellable
* Multiple Products can refer to the same Inventory
* Allows you to set an Inventory item, sale quantity, total price, a weight, and dimensions  (so you can create a Product for 1x NEMA17 and 5x NEMA17 that's cheaper than 5x1)

**OnDemandProduct**
* Not associated with any Inventory
* Same pricing / weight / dimension rules as Physical, so a Shipping module can account for it

@Brett - KB3D  -- am i missing any types of Product?


[8/4/2025 1:44 PM] instazx2
The way I envision Backorders working is with the events notifying carts-in-progress of inventory being sold:

1) When a cart is sold, it will broadcast several different messages, but one of them is a "InventoryQuantityReduced" message
2) This message is picked up by the Inventory module, which subtracts the quantity, and notifies the Products about this change
3) Any Product for this Inventory will analyse the new quantity, and conditionally make themselves Available
4) Any Product that is now Available then produces a "ProductQuantityUpdated" event

5) Everyone else's carts immediately pick up this event, and compare the quantity in their cart with the quantity that was just announced in that event
6) Any carts with less quantity than available are unaffected, and proceed as normal
7) Any carts with more quantity than is remaining will go into the Backorder workflow
8) Backordered carts will check the Backorder rules for all their Products
9) If all Products in the cart allow backordering, then the Customer is shown a notification on the site that a Product in their Cart is out of stock but can be backordered

10) If the Customer decides to backorder their Cart, the checkout process goes through as normal, but the Cart is held in a "Pending" state
11) The "CartStatusPending" event can be used to notify you that someone backordered something
12) When the Inventory is restocked, the backoffice portal generates an "InventoryQuantityIncreased" message
13) This message is picked up by the Inventory module, which changes itself from Backordered to Available
14) The Product module produces a "ProductQuantityUpdated" event
15) This message propagates down to all the current Carts, and if any of them are backordered for this Product, then they are woken back up

16) All Carts in the "Pending" state immediately try and complete their checkout again, restarting back at Step #1


[8/4/2025 1:50 PM] instazx2
this gives us:

* No negative inventory quantity ever
* Inventory that can backorder itself if the quantity hits a threshold
* Products that have no quantity, like the acrylic panels
* Prioritizing backordered carts when stock becomes available
* Allows/Disallows backordering at an Inventory, Product, and Customer level
* Carts keep a local copy of the available Product quantities, so there's less DB overhead during high traffic
* Allows for carts to "sort" themselves when stock is getting low
* Allows a backordered Cart to be later cancelled without impacting stock quantity

[8/5/2025 5:42 PM] instazx2
like ... ugh, god, fuck.   "Wire Harness + BOM kit for Voron V2.4 & Trident".  there's 309 of those fucking things, because of all the combinations and modifications.


[8/5/2025 5:43 PM] instazx2

[8/5/2025 5:46 PM] instazx2
you shouldn't have to track a fucktillion permutations of that 'stock' either


[8/5/2025 5:48 PM] instazx2
what *should* that look like, though?  (definitely a @Brett - KB3D question too)


[8/5/2025 5:54 PM] instazx2
i would expect the printed parts to be an **OnDemandProduct**.  that wouldnt' prevent you from keeping some sets pre-printed if you wanted, but it wouldn't matter to the software.

is this where 'variants' come in?  like, a V2.4 kit is:

* frame
* motors
* electronics
* motion
* rails
* bed
* toolhead
* panels

do we make 3 of them, for the 3x BOM sizes?  each one would have either the universal sub-kit (motors / toolhead / electronics) or one-of-three versions of the sub-kit (frame, bed, rails, motion)


[8/5/2025 6:09 PM] instazx2
maybe we introduce a concept of "requires / provides" for kits?

this way you'd possibly have 3 different "V2.4 kit", by size.  we put 8x "requires"-shaped holes into it, and the system can use the "provides" to find a match?

```
{
  "name": "V2.4 kit - 250mm",
  "requires": [
    { "name": "frame_voron_250", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "motor_nema17", "quantity": 6 },
    { "name": "electronics_large", "quantity:": 1},
    { "name": "motion_voron_250", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "rails_voron_250", "quantity": 1 },
    etc
  ],
  "provides": "voron_v24_250"
}
```


[8/5/2025 6:09 PM] instazx2
then, lets consider the electronics.  maybe you made your own categories -- "electronics_small" for <= 5 motors, "electronics_large" for > 5 motors.  the 2.4 - 250 kit "requires" some combination of parts to satisfy "electronics_large", so lets look at possibilities:

```
{
  "name": "Dual SKR 1.4",
  "requires": [
    { "name": "skr_14", "quantity": 2 },
    { "name": "sbc", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "skr_din_clip", "quantity": 2 },
    { "name": "stepstick", "quantity": 8 },
    etc
  ],
  "provides": [ "electronics_large", "electronics_small" ]
},
{
  "name": "BTT Octopus (Pro)",
  "requires": [
    { "name": "btt_octopus", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "sbc", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "btt_octopus_din", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "stepstick", "quantity": 8 }
  ],
  "provides": [ "electronics_large", "electronics_small" ]
},
{
  "name": "BTT Manta M8P",
  "requires": [
    { "name": "manta_m8p", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "manta_din_clip", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "stepstick", "quantity": 8 }
  ],
  "provides": [ "electronics_large", "electronics_small" ]
}
```


[8/5/2025 6:13 PM] instazx2
in this model, those 3 electronics products themselves requires/provides.  somebody choosing "Dual SKR" or "BTT Octopus" would get a dropdown for "sbc", where they could choose Pi3B+, Pi4, Pi5, OrangePi, whatever.  those would show up because those two products need some selection of "sbc" in order to be purchasable.  the Manta M8P doesn't have a requirement for "sbc" since it uses an onboard CM4 / CB1.  the M8P might instead use "sbc_module" so you could separately list the CM4 / CB1 as two additional products that 'provide' "sbc_module"


[8/5/2025 6:17 PM] instazx2
also 
```
{
  "name": "(none)",
  "requires": [],
  "provides": [ "electronics_large", "electronics_small" ]
}
```

would let us have a (none) option when the user is configuring something.  keep adding more 'provides' options so it shows up in more and more dropdowns.  since it has an empty "requires" list, any option where they chose "(none)" would 'stop' the chain of selection.  since it itself doesn't require anything, there's no more choices to make.  since there's no more choices to make, there's no link between this *part* of the kit and any purchasable Products / Inventory


[8/5/2025 6:24 PM] instazx2
would allow for something like a `VariantProduct`:
```
{
  "name": "Voron 250mm frame",
  "variants": [
    { "name": "Black", "sku": "voron_250_frame_black", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "Blue", "sku": "voron_250_frame_blue", "quantity": 1 },
    { "name": "Orange", "sku": "voron_250_frame_orange", "quantity": 1 },
    etc
  ],
  "provides": [ "frame_voron_250" ]
}
```

and now each of those finally references a `PhysicalProduct`:
```
{
  "name": "Voron 250 frame (black)",
  "sku": "voron_250_frame_black",
  "inventory": {
    "partNumber": "voron_250_frame_black",
    "stockQuantity": 191
  },
  "quantity": 1,
  "itemPrice": 64.99,
  "weight": 3800,  // grams
  "length": 45,    // centimeters
  "width": 12,
  "height": 12
}
```


[8/5/2025 6:26 PM] instazx2
the voron_250_frame_black PhysicalProduct could be the default Product that's auto-created when you add a new Inventory item, to cut down on manual work of duplicating those.  (this is suggesting you have 191 individual kits of black voron 250 frames in stock)

what this does though is when a particular color of frame kit goes out of stock / discontinued / whatever, it's no longer available to ultimately end up as a "provides" option.  which means it just stops showing up on kits, but it doesn't fuck with all the other stock levels or permutations


[8/5/2025 6:26 PM] instazx2
that also means you could share frame kits between v2.4 and tridents (i think)

[8/6/2025 11:29 AM] instazx2
i have this in C#, and it works
```csharp
private async Task CreateInventoryAsync()
{
    var item = new CreateInventoryPayload
    {
        Description = "NEMA17 Motor",
        PartNumber = "MOTOR_NEMA17",
        StockQuantity = 8000
    };

    await ApiClient.Inventory.PostAsync(item);
}
```

like, if you can figure out how to get "NEMA17 Motor" and "8000" from someone using a website you control, this is all you need to interact with the actual "event bus database access masstransit saga fully distributed whatever" backend


[8/6/2025 11:29 AM] instazx2

[8/7/2025 8:53 AM] brett4091
```
Current e-commerce variant systems are often rigid and limited - typically handling simple attributes like size, color, and material with basic combinations. Here's what I'd propose for a more sophisticated approach:

Core Architecture Improvements

Hierarchical Variant Structure: Instead of flat attribute lists, use a tree structure where variants can have parent-child relationships. For example, "Material" could branch into "Cotton" which then branches into "Organic Cotton" and "Regular Cotton."
Dynamic Attribute System: Allow merchants to define custom attributes with validation rules, dependencies, and display logic. Attributes could have types (text, numeric, boolean, enum, range) with appropriate UI controls.

Conditional Logic Engine: Implement rules that show/hide certain combinations based on other selections. If someone picks "Leather" as material, automatically hide color options that don't make sense for leather.

Advanced Features

Multi-dimensional Variants: Support for complex products like configurable software, custom manufacturing, or services where variants aren't just physical attributes but could include duration, capacity, or feature sets.

Inventory Forecasting Integration: The variant system should predict demand for combinations and suggest which variants to stock based on historical data and current trends.
Visual Variant Builder: A drag-and-drop interface where merchants can visually map out variant relationships, set up rules, and preview how customers will experience the selection process.
Smart Defaults and Recommendations: Use ML to suggest the most popular or relevant variant combinations for each customer based on their browsing history, location, or similar customer behavior.

Technical Implementation

Graph Database Backend: Store variants and their relationships in a graph structure rather than traditional relational tables, allowing for more flexible querying and relationship mapping.
Real-time Validation: As customers select options, instantly validate availability, pricing changes, and compatibility without page reloads.
Headless API Design: Separate the variant logic from presentation, allowing the same system to power web, mobile, AR try-on experiences, and voice commerce.

The key insight is moving from "products with variants" to "configurable product systems" - treating each product as a small decision tree that can adapt to different business models and customer needs.
```

[8/7/2025 9:20 AM] instazx2
idk how much you looked at the 'backend' code.  i know we spent an hour trying to get it to run, and the end result of it running was it immediately breaking ... so that wasn't exactly a great showing.


[8/7/2025 9:22 AM] instazx2
but of the backend code -- about half of all of it is covered by tests.  of that half, about half again doesn't need to be covered by automated tests (since it's like configuration or startup code, not business logic) -- but that still leaves about 1/4 of all the codebase that doesnt' have automated testing


[8/7/2025 9:22 AM] instazx2
and, directly in that 1/4 of the codebase -- the places that were breaking yesterday with the 500 Server Errors


[8/7/2025 9:24 AM] instazx2
so one of the things i wanted to add was full, proper, "end-to-end" tests.  a complete standalone application that does the same `docker run` to boot up the whole stack, and then directly interacts with the API endpoints to create fake inventory, create fake products from that inventory, add things to a cart, etc.


[8/7/2025 9:25 AM] instazx2
right now, i've got verifications at each individual step.  like, "when you call the `GET /inventory` endpoint, the code calls `InventoryManager.GetInventory()`".  then another one that says "when you call `InventoryManager.GetInventory()`, then it will call `_database.Query()`".  then another one that says "when you call `_database.Query()`, you get back these items ..."


[8/7/2025 9:25 AM] instazx2
and each of those individual A->B, B->C, C->D checks are passing.  but when we *integrate* it all together for A->D, there's still more bugs


[8/7/2025 9:27 AM] instazx2
so by having a complete standalone application with a bunch of hardcoded steps to act as an automated version of someone interacting with a backoffice, we have single-step verification of the entire process together


[8/7/2025 9:28 AM] instazx2
and that's why i wanted to make the Kiota clients, regardless of the language.  that api client means i can write a test application to use all the functionality of this entire backend of code -- and if you (royal you) use the same api client, then not only can you reference my tests as examples, but there's automated proof that it does exactly what it says it does


[8/7/2025 9:36 AM] instazx2
if you're just looking at these big OpenAPI JSON documents, it doesn't give you any real place to start.  but with these automated tests, there's code like:

```cs
public class InventoryManagement_Tests
{
  private Guid InventoryId;

  public async Task CheckAll()
  {
    await TestCreate();
    await TestUpdate();
    // ...    
  }

  public async Task TestCreate()
  {
    var client = new ApiClient();

    // create a new item
    InventoryId = await client.Inventory.Create(new() {
      PartNumber = "FAST_M3X20",
      Description = "M3 Fastener",
      StockQuantity = 10000
    });

    // verify it exists
    var item = await client.Inventory.Get(InventoryId);
    if (item is null)
      throw new Exception("Create() failed");
  }

  // ...
}
```

or the same in PHP:
```php
class InventoryManagement_Tests {
    private $InventoryId;

    public function checkAll() {
        $this->testCreate();
        $this->testUpdate();
        // ...
    }

    public function testCreate() {
        $client = new ApiClient();

        // create a new item
        $this->InventoryId = $client->inventory->create([
            'partNumber' => 'FAST_M3X20',
            'description' => 'M3 Fastener',
            'stockQuantity' => 10000
        ]);

        // verify it exists
        $item = $client->inventory->get($this->InventoryId);
        if ($item === null) {
            throw new Exception("Create() failed");
        }
    }

    // ...
}
```

[8/7/2025 2:53 PM] brett4091
Take this for example. We have 2 "attributes" assigned, 3D Printer and Type. This gives us 4 variant combinations...

- A1 Mini | Glacier
- A1 Mini | Frostbite
- A1, P1, X1 | Glacier
- A1, P1, X1 | Frostbite


[8/7/2025 2:54 PM] brett4091
Each one of those combinations is assigned a unique SKU and EAN


[8/7/2025 2:55 PM] brett4091
By default, they inherit the retail price, wholesale cost, weight, etc. from the parent product, but we can adjust those values at the variant combination level if need be

[8/8/2025 10:14 AM] instazx2
@Brett - KB3D  / @Kbreezy - KB3D  ... either of you have opinions about "provides" / "requires" for kit configurators?


[8/8/2025 11:09 AM] instazx2
@Brett - KB3D  for the combinatorial variants (basically the discussion yesterday), would you suggest we model that as an actual parent/child relationship?  (rigid coupling)  or a "copy to new..." (very loose, just duplicates the state as-is).

parent/child would make the ancillary properties fall through ... Description, Dimensions, Weight, Price.  the two are inexorably linked, although it could possibly be one-time broken via "copy to new...".  i guess a "combine with..." would also work.  it does introduce grossness with "how deep do we let products be nested".  the easiest way of an optional `ParentProductId` field immediately makes all products potentially infinitely nestable, as well as creating circular references  (A -> B -> C -> A, so the code to "get the parent description" would loop forever)

only supporting "copy to new..." would prevent all the edge-cases around hierarchical products, but it makes it easier for things to get out of sync.


[8/8/2025 11:26 AM] instazx2
actually, this is just "tags".  so it's more speced like:
```js
{
  "id": "5b0e3fc9-ec75-4234-bb24-8648e2605974"

  "requires": [
```

[8/8/2025 12:12 PM] brett4091
When an order is placed, it generally comes through as either "Payment Accepted" which means ready to ship, or "Awaiting Payment" where it's still pending confirmation from whatever processor.

The shipping software (ShipStation) basically runs a cron every X minutes, using the PrestaShop webservice to fetch all "Payment Accepted" orders and pulling them in for fulfillment. The packing slip, shipping label, etc. are all handled by ShipStation rather than at the store level. Once shipped, it sends a notification back to PrestaShop and the status changes to "Shipped".

Volumetric estimations would be helpful for calculating the correct shipping rates at checkout. Our current UPS / USPS rate modules use very, and I mean *very* rough estimations. It's pretty accurate for single products, but this can result in the customer paying more or less for multi-product orders. Not uncommon but definitely frustrating, especially when they pay $20 for shipping but it costs us $40 to send it. ShipStation offers live rates for a lot of e-commerce platforms, but PrestaShop is not one of those.

An inventory map / picklist is absolutely something Kris has been interested in, and would love to have!

Kris is for sure the one to answer any questions about kit configurators and what he wants from them. He had that entire system custom built to his exact specifications.


[8/8/2025 12:14 PM] instazx2
i did not realize he had it custom made


[8/8/2025 12:14 PM] instazx2

[8/8/2025 12:15 PM] brett4091
Yeah, if memory serves, he couldn't find anyone to take that behemoth of a project on, but finally found this guy who was willing to tackle it


[8/8/2025 12:15 PM] brett4091
It has definitely become a core part of the store and makes the shopping experience a lot easier for customers, as opposed to piecing stuff together from dozens of different listings

[8/8/2025 2:37 PM] instazx2
there's 2 domains so far:

* Catalog
* * does everything related to backend management of *physical* products, either stock-backed or on-demand
* * does not care about prices, only physical properties.  maybe tags live here?

* Storefront
* * has an arbitrary number of "SellableItems", which primarily get created via events burbling out of Catalog
* * could create "SellableItems" that are not backed by Products at all, like gift cards or PDFs
* * primarily responsible for controlling the "read" part of using the site


[8/8/2025 2:38 PM] instazx2
(which also means 'maintenance mode' could leave the site in a read-only mode that's otherwise fully functional)


[8/8/2025 2:40 PM] brett4091

[8/8/2025 2:55 PM] instazx2
yeah, trying to isolate concerns into their own domains is simplifying some things and making others really weird


[8/8/2025 2:58 PM] instazx2
like, anything with "customers and carts" will probably be an entirely separate area again


[8/8/2025 3:03 PM] instazx2
and "customers" might itself be separate from "carts".  like, the carts could be quarantined off into their own area, living in Redis.  incredibly fast, no database load.  on some kind of background operation, a logged-in user's cart could sync to their profile, but anonymous users wouldn't.  

we then won't need to spend any database resources on abandoned or in-progress carts.  they just get persisted to disk for carts we care about


[8/8/2025 3:10 PM] instazx2
like, Redis allows setting an expiration on an entry.

* someone visits site
* if guest, they get a completely random "accountId"
* if logged in, they reuse their original "accountId"
* we create a Cart with the same 'accountId' in Redis, and give it a 30 minute expiration
* any time the Cart is modified, the 30 minute timer restarts again
* as long as they're on the site, it'd do little pings in the background to keep the Cart alive
* if they actually CheckOut, the Cart gets persisted to a different spot with long-term retention
* if they close and abandon the Cart, then after 30 minutes it just quietly poofs itself from existence
* there's absolutely no "remove abandoned carts" maintenance we'd ever have to do

[8/12/2025 11:18 AM] instazx2
so i have `Inventory` with "PartNumber" + "StockQuantity".

i have multiple `Products` tied to a single `InventoryId` -- the differences being "Quantity", "Price", "Dimensions", "Weight", and made unique by "Sku".  each `Product` has an optional, customizable threshold for "StockThreshold" as well, but the default if not set is the "Quantity".

lets say there's 10 widgets in Inventory, and 3 Products:

* Widget 1pk - $3.99  threshold not set
* Widget 5pk - $9.99  threshold 8
* Widget 10pk - $14.99 threshold not set

at this instant in time, all 3 Widgets are "Available" because the current Inventory is above their threshold.

if somebody buys the 5pk of Widgets, is this the correct behavior across the board?

* ✨  magic happens elsewhere ✨  -> `DecreaseInventoryQuantityRequest` -> "Widget -5"
* "Inventory" loads Widget, sees qty10, subtracts 5, replies "Success" to the request, and publishes `InventoryQuantityChanged { Id=Widget, Qty=5 }`
* `InventoryQuantityChanged` -> "Products WHERE Product.InventoryId = Message.InventoryId"
* "Widget 1pk" -> min threshold 1 (for 1pk, no custom threshold set) -> no change
* "Widget 5pk" -> min threshold 8 (custom set to 8) -> `ProductAvailabilityChanged { Id=Widget-5pk, IsAvailable=False }`
* "Widget 10pk" -> min threshold 10 (for 10pk, no custom set) -> `ProductAvailabilityChanged { Id=Widget-10pk, IsAvailable=False }`

* User cart with "Widget 5pk" is now marked "Invalid" or something, so it can't start (or continue) a checkout process

--- [time passes] ---

Kris either buys more Widgets, or counts the ones he has.  Either way, it turns out there's a total of 9 available instead of 5:

* `SetInventoryQuantityRequest` -> "Widget =9"
* "Inventory" loads Widget, sees qty5, forces it to 9, replies "Success" to the request, and publishes `InventoryQuantityChanged { Id=Widget, Qty=9 }`
* `InventoryQuantityChanged` -> "Products WHERE ..."
* "Widget 1pk" -> still fine, no change
* "Widget 5pk" -> 9 is above the custom threshold of 8, `ProductAvailabilityChanged { Id=Widget-5pk, IsAvailable=True }`
* "Widget 10pk" -> 9 still below threshold of 10, no change

* User cart with "Widget 5pk" now becomes "Valid" or something, so it now can start (or continue) a checkout process


[8/12/2025 11:24 AM] brett4091
Aside from a threshold of 8 for a 5 pack, all of the logic here is sound


[8/12/2025 11:24 AM] instazx2
idk maybe it's something that sells fast so you want the 5pk to become unavailable while there's still a few left for onesey-twosey carts?

[8/12/2025 11:27 AM] instazx2

[8/12/2025 11:28 AM] instazx2

[8/12/2025 11:46 AM] instazx2

[8/12/2025 11:47 AM] brett4091

[8/12/2025 11:48 AM] brett4091

[8/12/2025 11:50 AM] brett4091
That doesn't *always* mean it has been paid for yet, but the order being created effectively puts it on reserve. If we don't receive payment within a given timeframe, the order is cancelled and the item(s) are restocked


[8/12/2025 11:52 AM] instazx2

[8/12/2025 11:54 AM] instazx2

[8/12/2025 12:00 PM] brett4091

[8/12/2025 12:01 PM] brett4091

[8/12/2025 12:03 PM] brett4091

[8/12/2025 12:03 PM] brett4091
This seemed to make a huge difference, where we went from seeing a bunch of "Payment Error" orders and having to contact them directly, to instead having the customer following up immediately after with a successful order


[8/12/2025 12:03 PM] brett4091

[8/12/2025 12:08 PM] instazx2

[8/12/2025 12:12 PM] brett4091

[8/12/2025 12:22 PM] instazx2 (pinned)
but the conversion of Cart to Order (maybe this is what happens when they click "Checkout" ?), could do something like:

* create new Order, set the Customer information
* loop over every SellableItem in the cart, call `Purchase(itemId, quantity, cartId, orderId)` for each one
* the `Purchase` method takes the `itemId + quantity`, looks up the SellableItem, and if it's tied to a `Product` then it sends `DecreaseInventoryQuantityRequest` with the total quantity to each `Inventory` item
* the Inventory returns either success or some kind of failure for each one (either not found, or insufficient stock).  assuming everything was happy, each `DecreaseInventoryQuantityResponse` that comes back will have the price at that exact instant, and that's what gets baked into the order line items
* as these responses come back, the SellableItem is removed from the Cart and added to the Order.  this is where the queues come in handy 🙂
* eventually all the responses come back, if every single one was successful then the Cart is removed and the Order moves on
* if one or more of the responses comes back with an error, we can show it to the user right then for them to make a decision.
* if they remove the item from the cart and continue, then the Cart and Order match and it moves on
* if allowed and they choose to Backorder, then ... a flag gets set on the Order indicating Backordered or something?
* regardless, Order is now just about ready for payment.  we do any coupon / discount code / price rules now in the background, then generate an Invoice to attach to the Order
* user is taken to the Payment page, makes a Payment, Payment is used to satisfy the Invoice.
* Invoice turns around and harasses the Order, which moves to the "Awaiting Shipping"

{Reactions}
💯

Sounds like pretty standard steps meshed into your objects / methods!

[8/12/2025 12:32 PM] instazx2
the way the StateMachines / queues work is a big birdshot of "Decrease Inventory" messages fly at the Inventory domain and land in the queues immediately.  MassTransit runs multiple instances of the consumers, so it consumes dozens of messages at a time. when it picks up an Inventory message, the first thing it does is puts a lock on that item in the database, does the processing, updates the database, then releases the lock.

if there's multiple messages at once for the same Inventory item, the first one that makes it to the database by even a few microseconds gets the lock, and the others *for that item* wait their turn.  any messages that are for a different item process in parallel.

so we simultaneously get high concurrency and parallelism, while preventing race conditions


[8/12/2025 12:33 PM] brett4091
All my homies hate race conditions


[8/12/2025 12:33 PM] instazx2
but that's how it ensures that the counts stay consistent even with multiple orders happening simultaneously, which i hope prevents the need for the soft-reservations


[8/12/2025 12:34 PM] brett4091
Yeah, it sounds like this would be more than capable of handling the cart/ordering process without any sort of reservation type system


[8/12/2025 12:34 PM] brett4091
I think with most e-commerce platforms, reservations are really only used in that short space between order placed -> order shipped.


[8/12/2025 12:35 PM] brett4091
The physical item hasn't actually left the building yet, but someone has paid (or at least intends to pay) for it, so it is placed into the reserve status briefly


[8/12/2025 12:37 PM] instazx2
idk if that unasked-for queues explanation helps with concerns about how it should behave under more intense scenarios
