[8/12/2025 12:54 PM] brett4091
I think this PrestoSearch module is more or less ready to go live. The monthly "You are over your account limit and will be paying more again" email is always a bit triggering.

However, the machine learning capabilities of DooFinder do make the direct MySQL queries (even with the fancy aggregate table) feel a little lackluster on the results. Mostly in cases of mistypes. For example, "NH36" and "NH-36", with one returning the Nitehawk 36 product and the other finding nothing. With the basic analytics we can slowly improve that over time, identifying what comes back with no results, but it would be ideal to be proactive on that front.

We'd much rather pay the DooFinder fee than end up with worse search results, why I haven't pushed this live yet.


[8/12/2025 12:55 PM] brett4091
It also hasn't been a main priority though, so it's pretty much where we left it last time, with the aggregate table implemented but that's it


[8/12/2025 12:55 PM] brett4091

Rust offers memory safety without garbage collection through its ownership system, eliminating entire classes of bugs like buffer overflows and data races at compile time. It provides zero-cost abstractions and fine-grained control over system resources, making it ideal for performance-critical applications. While Go prioritizes simplicity and fast compilation for network services and concurrent programming, Rust excels in systems programming, embedded development, and scenarios where maximum performance and safety are paramount.

like the Blazor grid thing yesterday.  when you've got one of the DataGrids, there's an "ItemsProvider" you can set that the grid calls when it wants to fetch data.


[8/13/2025 3:02 PM] instazx2
but this is how we can quickly work around the API client missing calls (because the API itself is missing the feature to call)

```csharp
<FluentDataGrid 
  TGridItem="InventoryModel"
  ItemsProvider="QueryInventoryAsync">

  <FluentGridColumn> ... </FluentGridColumn>
</FluentDataGrid>

@code {

private async ValueTask<GridItemsProviderResult<InventoryModel>> QueryInventoryAsync()
{
  // ideal code, assuming it's supported.  get the real data from the backend:
  // var results = await apiClient.Inventory.GetAll();
  // return GridItemsProviderResult.From(results.Data, results.TotalItems);

  // fake hacky hardcoded result:
  var results = new List<InventoryModel>
  {
    new() { PartNumber = "ABC_123", Description = "Test item", StockQuantity = 50 },
    new() { PartNumber = "DEF_456", Description = "Something else", StockQuantity = 5 }
  }

  return GridItemsProviderResult.From(results, 5);
}

}
```


[8/13/2025 3:03 PM] instazx2
that's a way i can think of to get something nearly immediate to start playing with, to do the layouts and such with.  i'm hoping that by starting to scratch an actual UI together, the desired workflow falls out the bottom

[8/13/2025 3:25 PM] instazx2

if you make a mockup using the real FluentDataGrid with a fake provider method like i hacked above, that will be much easier to plug into, but there will be more up-front overhead trying to learn and fight the UI framework.

if we make fake client, or real client + fake API, then there is no "plug in after the fact" because your part is done

[8/13/2025 8:02 PM] instazx2
@Brett - KB3D   is there a use-case for changing the underlying Inventory for Products?  right now it's set-on-creation, and you can't change it after the Product is created.  that feels safest to me for long-term data integrity, but potentially adds extra steps


[8/13/2025 8:04 PM] brett4091
How would restocking be handled? Or manual adjustments?


[8/13/2025 8:05 PM] instazx2
StockQuantity is a property of Inventory, so we'd manipulate that particular value


[8/13/2025 8:06 PM] instazx2
i can't think of a good case to link to a different Inventory after creating a Product.  the Inventory doesn't really have much associated with it, it's literally just 'part number', 'status', and 'stock quantity'


[8/13/2025 8:07 PM] instazx2
all the weights, dimensions, prices, human-readable descriptions, etc are all at the Product level


[8/13/2025 8:08 PM] brett4091
Makes sense. I can't think of any reason to change the underlying Inventory then


[8/13/2025 8:08 PM] instazx2
yeah i really can't either.  it's relevant because of the deletion / disabling questions


[8/13/2025 8:17 PM] instazx2
so we've got Inventory, linked to 3 products (just different quantities or something).  Kris decides fuck selling this, i'm never selling this again, and he goes to the Inventory page and clicks "Delete".

backend sends `DeleteInventoryRequest { Id=xxxxx }` to the saga.  saga gets the message, looks up the Id, sees that it's currently "Enabled", and moves to the "Discontinued" state.  this sends a message that immediately disables the 3 related Products so they can't be sold anymore.  any carts with that item get frozen and can't be fulfilled until all the items are valid again.

everything sits in this Discontinued state, for reporting purposes.  if we then send `DeleteInventoryRequest { Id=xxxxx }` to the now-Discontinued item, it will get *actually* fully properly deleted out of the database.  it goes away, you lose all history of it, the PartNumber can be reused, etc.  it's *gone* gone.  because the Product -> Inventory link can't change, what should happen to the Products who's Inventory is gone forever and also can't be updated?


[8/13/2025 8:20 PM] instazx2
the only reason i can think of for double-deleting an Inventory item is if you typo'ed the partnumber or something when creating it.  PartNumber is the only thing that can't change after you create an item.

Discontinued / Deleted doesn't mean that deleted items must show up in the middle of the current screen and be obnoxious.  they could be easily hidden, archived, etc so they just remain soft-deleted like Discontinued.  this is more the data-model behind the scenes.


[8/13/2025 8:21 PM] instazx2
i'm thinking any related Products themselves just get whole-ass deleted if you double-delete the Inventory item


[8/13/2025 8:21 PM] instazx2
if you're double-deleting, you have fucked something up so badly that it's easier across the board to start over than try and fix it


[8/13/2025 8:25 PM] instazx2
we do need some kind of un-Discontinue operation.  should we 'wake up' a Discontinued Inventory item by sending another "CreateInventory" with the same part number?  that's supposed to fail (because it would be a dupe) but in the case where the Inventory is just discontinued but not deleted, that could be a pretty clean way to wake it back up.  it would re-enable all the downstream Products.  it would not un-delete anything, which is why deletion is a multi-step process so it's not accidental


[8/13/2025 8:29 PM] instazx2
Inventory also has Hold/Release, which is still most akin to "Lock/Unlock" as opposed to the intent of "reserved for sale".  it also supports Backordered, which is automatically started/ended based on StockQuantity.

Product tracks "In Stock" based on the underlying Inventory (on-demand stuff is always In Stock).
Products can also be Enabled / Disabled, as an administrative flag.

whether a product is *Available* is the combination of "In Stock" + "Enabled".  either of those being false means the Product can't be sold.

[8/22/2025 10:31 AM] instazx2
the "TagType" is an enum that i control.  right now i have a few things in it:   `Association = 1, Location = 2`.

if a Device gets transferred to a Location, then i'll add a new entry in the "DeviceTags" table.  the TagId will be set to the LocationId insetad, and TagType will be 2.  if a Device gets Associated to a User, then i'll add a second DeviceTag row, and the TagId will be the UserId instead (with TagType 1)


[8/22/2025 10:33 AM] instazx2
gives me easy ways to look at historical changes of *relationships*, like UserId associations or LocationId associations


[8/22/2025 10:34 AM] instazx2

* Update Device AssetTag
* Update Device Role  (is it an admin device, user device, demo device, etc)
* A few similar things

these changes aren't necessarily a "relationship", more like an audit record.  I'd handle them with INSERT/DELETE into the "read-only" tables, and then publishing a "ThingHappened" event afterwards that i can hook to do my tracking & reporting

[8/22/2025 10:43 AM] instazx2
there's a few options i can think of:

create a few hardcoded objects that are different tag types?

```cs
public interface IDeviceTag
{
  Guid TagId { get; }
  TagTypes TagType { get; }
}

public class LocationUpdatedTag : IDeviceTag
{
  public static LocationUpdatedTag FromLocation(LocationModel model) => new() { TagId = model.LocationId };

  public Guid TagId { get; set; }
  public TagTypes TagType { get; } = TagTypes.Location;
}

public class DeviceAssociationTag : IDeviceTag
{
  public static DeviceAssociationTag FromUser(UserModel model) => new() { TagId = model.UserId };

  public Guid TagId { get; set; }
  public TagTypes TagType { get; } = TagTypes.Assignment;
}
// more for other relational types

// audit-type tags?
public class AssetTagUpdatedTag : IDeviceTag
{
  public Guid TagId { get; } = Guid.Parse("<hardcoded guid 1>");
  public TagTypes TagType { get; } = TagTypes.AssetTag;
}

public class DeviceLostTag : IDeviceTag
{
  public Guid TagId { get; } = Guid.Parse("<hardcoded guid 2>");
  public TagTypes TagType { get; } = TagTypes.DeviceLost;
}
```

and this way anytime one of those behaviors happened to a device, it would be added to the table with that "fixed" ID


[8/22/2025 10:43 AM] instazx2
that doesn't really break the model.  the DeviceTags table doesn't care what the UUID actually is, it's used by whoever cares about the association to understand how to get the human-readable value

[8/22/2025 11:01 AM] brett4091
```
I think your Hybrid Event Store solution is really solid. You've identified the key issue - that your colleague is centralizing at the wrong abstraction level.
Instead of forcing property changes into the relationship model OR creating completely separate tables, your approach:
sql-- Keep the existing DeviceTags for relationships
CREATE TABLE DeviceTags (
  DeviceTagId UUID,
  DeviceId UUID,
  TagId UUID,
  TagType INT,
  Status INT,
  CreatedAt TIMESTAMP,
  RemovedAt TIMESTAMP
);

-- Your proposed unified event table that can reference DeviceTags OR stand alone
CREATE TABLE DeviceEvents (
  EventId UUID,
  DeviceId UUID,
  EventType INT,        -- RelationshipChange=1, PropertyChange=2, SystemAction=3
  RelatedTagId UUID,    -- References DeviceTags.DeviceTagId if applicable, NULL otherwise
  PropertyName VARCHAR(50),  -- For property changes
  OldValue VARCHAR(200),
  NewValue VARCHAR(200),
  EventData JSON,       -- For complex/flexible data
  CreatedAt TIMESTAMP,
  CreatedBy UUID
);
Why Your Approach Works Better
For Relationships:

DeviceTags entry created as usual
DeviceEvents entry created with EventType=1 and RelatedTagId pointing to the DeviceTags entry
Maintains their existing relationship logic while adding audit trail

For Property Changes:

DeviceEvents entry with EventType=2, RelatedTagId=NULL
Clean, queryable structure for property history
No forced semantics or magic GUIDs

For System Actions:

DeviceEvents entry with EventType=3 for things like "notification sent"
Uses EventData JSON field for flexible structure

Your Key Insight
The colleague's instinct about centralization is right, but they're centralizing at the wrong level. Your solution centralizes through DeviceEvents while keeping DeviceTags focused on what it does best - managing relationships.
Query Examples
sql-- Complete device history (unified view)
SELECT * FROM DeviceEvents WHERE DeviceId = @deviceId ORDER BY CreatedAt;

-- Just relationship changes
SELECT e.*, t.TagId, t.TagType 
FROM DeviceEvents e 
JOIN DeviceTags t ON e.RelatedTagId = t.DeviceTagId 
WHERE e.DeviceId = @deviceId AND e.EventType = 1;

-- Just property changes
SELECT * FROM DeviceEvents 
WHERE DeviceId = @deviceId AND EventType = 2 
ORDER BY PropertyName, CreatedAt;
Your approach gives them their "single source of truth" while maintaining semantic clarity and avoiding architectural gymnastics. The magic GUID approach feels too clever - your solution is cleaner and more maintainable.
Does this capture what you were thinking? The approach addresses both the architectural elegance your colleague wants and the practical maintainability concerns.
```

[9/8/2025 10:49 AM] brett4091
```
RewriteCond %{QUERY_STRING} q=Brand- [NC,OR]
RewriteCond %{QUERY_STRING} q=Availability- [NC,OR]
RewriteCond %{QUERY_STRING} q=Categories- [NC]
RewriteRule .* - [F,L]
```


[9/8/2025 10:49 AM] brett4091
That was all it took lol


[9/8/2025 10:49 AM] brett4091
Server load dropped from 10-20 down to about 0.1-0.2

@Brett - KB3D  i know how to make ML pipelines, train my own models, and now i'm learning algorithmic genetic optimization 🙂

{Reactions}
😮

[9/22/2025 2:25 PM] instazx2
if any of those would be useful in context of our other stuff


[9/22/2025 2:45 PM] brett4091
That is insanely awesome!


[9/22/2025 2:45 PM] brett4091

[9/22/2025 2:46 PM] brett4091
Problem was, 90% of the advice on there was garbage lol


[9/22/2025 2:46 PM] instazx2
oh man, that would be pretty tough


[9/22/2025 2:46 PM] instazx2
to be fair, the models i'm talking about are more "machine learning" rather than "llm"/"gpt"


[9/22/2025 2:46 PM] brett4091
What is algorithmic genetic optimization?


[9/22/2025 2:47 PM] instazx2
so things like pricing, stock levels, etc


[9/22/2025 2:48 PM] instazx2
it's a way to solve the kinds of problems that are otherwise a shitload of guess & check


[9/22/2025 2:48 PM] brett4091
Ah that's amazing


[9/22/2025 2:48 PM] instazx2
right now i'm using genetic algorithms to build regular expressions, the two are a really natural match


[9/22/2025 2:48 PM] instazx2
in this case, it's for parsing street addresses into their components


[9/22/2025 2:49 PM] instazx2
i have about 1.5 million raw address -> processed structured address pairs, so i can feed all 1.5 million "319 sw elm street" raws into my software, and tell it to extract "house number"


[9/22/2025 2:51 PM] instazx2
the structured response from Lob is what i'm using for the labelled components, it'll give me something like:

```js
{
  "street_primary": "319 SW ELM ST",
  "components": {
    "street_number": "319",
    "pre_direction": "SW",
    "street_name": "ELM",
    "post_direction": "",
    "designator": "ST"
  }
}
```


[9/22/2025 2:53 PM] instazx2
so now i have 1.5 million examples of `"319 sw elm street" should become '319'.  "N 2nd Main Ave" should become '2'.  "437 rockport dr unit 160" should become '437'`


[9/22/2025 2:54 PM] instazx2
the genetic process starts with a 'population' of about 100.  each one generates a random regex by slapping characters together and seeing if it throws an exception or not.  it randomly guesses regexes until i have 100 "valid" ones, but since they're just random characters it's a very incoherent mess


[9/22/2025 2:54 PM] instazx2
but then the framework takes each random regex, and runs it against my input training data


[9/22/2025 2:55 PM] instazx2
sometimes the regex, by pure accident, has some kind of element in it that makes it do a better job of matching the thing i'm looking for


[9/22/2025 2:56 PM] instazx2
like maybe it'll generate a `\d`, it's got a 1-in-10 chance or so.  well, \d is a really good grammar for "capture the numbers", since \d IS "capture a single digit"


[9/22/2025 2:57 PM] instazx2
this means the \d regex will get some hits in my training data -- any address with a single-digit street address.  the other randomized guesses might be things like `Q.13`, or `\s\s\s\s\sG` or whatever.  those are all going to have really bad matches


[9/22/2025 2:58 PM] instazx2
the framework then goes "ok all the ones that start with \d did pretty well, lets restart the process but each pattern will start with \d and then add the next random characters"


[9/22/2025 2:58 PM] instazx2
so now the second pass might have `\dx`, `\d{3}`, `\d+`, `\s\d`, etc.  these will have progressively better matches


[9/22/2025 2:59 PM] instazx2
it repeats the guess & check process, keeping the best ones from the previous run and using them as the baseline for the next one


[9/22/2025 2:59 PM] instazx2
they can converge on some absolutely *wild* regexes too, with startlingly good performance


[9/22/2025 3:00 PM] brett4091
This is absolutely wild. It's one thing to see LLMs that are confidently incorrect so often, compared to actual ML that actually improves


[9/22/2025 3:00 PM] instazx2
🙂


[9/22/2025 3:01 PM] instazx2
i'm mostly just playing with it now, trying to skill up


[9/22/2025 3:06 PM] instazx2
genetic algorithms are the kind of thing that really lets you optimize problems like "what's the most amount of stuff i can buy from aliexpress for $50 max, given this wishlist of search terms".  lets say the wishlist of items were jellybean parts, like 3dp hardware or similar.  many vendors on aliexpress would have the parts, but each vendor has the best price on just 1-2 things from your wishlist.  so you want to order the most from the cheapest vendor, until you exceed the cheap shipping thresholds or "small order" discounts.

you'd make the "score" for each choice be `(item_price_at_qty * item_qty) + store_total_shipping_price`, and each "generation" would be your wishlist items randomly chosen from multiple vendors.  the carts with the lowest score, likely all still above the $50 threshold, would be kept around for the second generation.  in the second generation, each cart would be randomly modified again -- replace this item from another vendor, increase the quantity of this, decrease the quantity of that, etc.  then the pricing rules on the site update each cart, you keep the cheapest ones, and repeat over and over.


[9/22/2025 3:08 PM] instazx2
they're nice in that they're **much** cheaper to build and maintain than LLMs or even standard ML models.  an ML model would be more like "given this purchase history, view history, search popularity, number of competing vendors for the same item, number of competing vendors for similar items, and 15 other datapoints --- what should this item be priced at?"


[9/22/2025 3:08 PM] brett4091
That is fascinating. I get overwhelmed when there are too many variables, at a certain point it breaks my brain trying to account for dozens of separate conditions, but it sounds like this could handle that extremely well


[9/22/2025 3:10 PM] brett4091
A few years back I tried to create a tool for fantasy football that could optimize the perfect lineup of players for that week. Take into account their historical performance each week, factor in opposing teams ranking against their position as a weight, same for their opponent this week, and a bunch of other things that could meaningfully contribute. I ended up with so many variables though, I was like nope, nope, nope, this is going in the trash.


[9/22/2025 3:11 PM] brett4091
There's plenty of lineup optimizers online but they are all generic crap. It would be interesting to see what ML could do in a case like that


[9/22/2025 3:11 PM] instazx2
yeah that would likely be a regression ML model

[9/23/2025 1:04 PM] instazx2

rinse/repeat 2-3x, then Claude has a perfect hierarchical TODO list with a bunch of tasks, and then it spirals off and builds everything


[9/23/2025 1:06 PM] instazx2
Opus is more than capable of understanding the actual details of the high-level TODO documents, but it's not good enough to come up with them all on its own.  but if me + Gemini talk through the high level stuff, then Gemini+Claude can manage the lower-level stuff

[10/28/2025 11:36 AM] instazx2
in addition to "role via prompt", you can also give specific sub-agents access to other directories.  i made one that i called `[library]-oracle`, and told it that it's the subject matter expert on [library], and gave it the source code as well. my other agents now ask it directly for how to do things, and it's incredibly optimized at researching answers and meta-prompting little task gophers to do it.  it's unbelievable how much Claude stopped sucking when i did this
